### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure: module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The demo module lattice has ZERO structural enforcement over this component. All three eslint `no-restricted-imports` objects encoding the demo module graph (G-DEMO-1/3a/3b) are keyed on `demo/@/**` globs matching zero files (`demo/@` was deleted at W43) and ban `@components/custom/...` specifiers behind an alias the same restructure removed. Additionally `vueCompilerOptions.strictTemplates` is unset everywhere, so vue-tsc never reports unknown component props.

**Mechanism.** structural invariants encoded against a tree layout that a later restructure moved; the lattice degraded to documentation without any gate noticing

**Evidence.**

```
`ls -d demo/@` → 'No such file or directory'. `find demo/@/components demo/@/lib demo/@/composables -type f | wc -l` → 0. eslint.config.js:232-238 and :274-276 (the dead globs); :286 (the dead @components pattern). tsconfig.demo.json:32-34 states the @components alias was killed. Measured effective config: `npx eslint --print-config src/value.ts` → inv-K-1 LIVE; `... demo/color-picker/App.vue` → G-DEMO-3b present but pattern targets dead alias (vacuous); `... demo/scenes/about/markdown/Markdown.vue` → UNSET/OFF; `... demo/scenes/about/markdown/composables/useMarkdownColors.ts` → UNSET/OFF. `grep -n 'strictTemplates' tsconfig*.json` → no match (vue-tsc default false).
```

**Reproduction.** cd /Users/mkbabb/Programming/value.js; npx eslint --print-config demo/scenes/about/markdown/Markdown.vue | python3 -c "import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['rules'].get('no-restricted-imports','UNSET/OFF'))"  # → UNSET/OFF ; ls -d demo/@  # → No such file or directory

**Proposed cure.** Re-key the three objects onto the real tree (demo/{color-session,palettes,picker,platform,scenes,shared,shell,workbenches}/**) and state the lattice as relative-depth bans — a scene leaf may not `../../../../` out of its scene. Enable vueCompilerOptions.strictTemplates. Both verifiable by `eslint --print-config` and one vue-tsc run; neither needs a design decision.

### `design` · D-1 · design

**Defect.** In forced-colors mode the slider track's extent is 1.00:1 against the well (invisible) and the focus indicator disappears entirely, because both are expressed in the single channel forced-colors overrides.

**Mechanism.** Single-channel control semantics: the track's entire visual definition routes through `background` (a custom property), which forced-colors overrides to Canvas; no border and no painted range remain as a second channel. Focus ships as box-shadow with outline-style:none, and forced-colors strips shadows. Violates VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:84 ("Focus remains visibly distinct ... forced colors") and WCAG 1.4.11. `grep -rn forced-colors demo/scenes/` returns zero hits.

**Evidence.**

```
Chromium forcedColors:"active", /#/atmosphere 1440x900 (scratchpad/chD-csp-probe2.mjs -> forcedFocus): trackBg rgb(255,255,255) === wellBg rgb(255,255,255), trackEqualsWell:true, trackBorderWidth 0px, rangeBg rgba(0,0,0,0); outlineStyle:"none", boxShadow:"none". Frame: scratchpad/chD-csp-forced-focus.png shows three orphan pill outlines with no track. Cause: ConfigSliderPane.vue:202 `--slider-track-bg: var(--ink-muted, var(--muted-foreground))`. The tracked matrix shots/forced-colors-desktop/blob.png could not catch this — WebKit ignores Playwright's forcedColors, so that frame is byte-comparable to the ordinary light frame.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/chD-csp-probe2.mjs (Chromium arm); or Chrome DevTools -> Rendering -> Emulate CSS forced-colors: active -> http://localhost:9000/#/atmosphere

**Proposed cure.** Fold into the F1 cure: retire variant="spectrum" so the producer's `.slider-range` paints the value, delete ConfigSliderPane.vue:202 and :204-206, and let glass-ui's Slider own a forced-colors arm — `border: 1px solid` on `.slider-track`, `.slider-range { background: Highlight; forced-color-adjust: none }`, and `outline` (not box-shadow) for :focus-visible. Producer change, not demo (edict 4).

---

### `design` · D-2 · design

**Defect.** Below 1024px the entire Blob configurator leaves the DOM; the sole purpose of /blob is unreachable at mobile widths and at 200% zoom, behind a global pane toggle the canon names a live defect.

**Mechanism.** The pane has no narrow arm of its own and inherits the shell's global pane toggle. OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:115 names a frame showing a "global pane toggle" a live defect; VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:32 law 6 says "no global pane selector ... survives". The pane's other consumer (/atmosphere) DOES render at 390px (rowCount 3), so one generic pane has two divergent, unspecified narrow behaviours.

**Evidence.**

```
WebKit breakpoint sweep on /#/blob (scratchpad/chD-csp-probe3.mjs -> breakpointSweep): 1440/1280/1100/1024 => .config-console present, 31 rows; 1000/900/768/600/390 => absent, 0 rows. Corroborated: REPORT.json safari-mobile-light /#/blob bodyTextLength 69, allElements 228 (vs 713/642 desktop), smallTapTargets limited to L/A/B/ALPHA channel. shots/safari-mobile-light/blob.png and shots/zoom-200-desktop/blob.png both show the Picker plus a Dock `Picker | Blob` toggle with Picker selected. visual/states.mjs:21-22 captures zoom-200 as a 720x450 viewport at DPR 2.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/blob at 1000px viewport width; or at 1440px with browser zoom 200%

**Proposed cure.** The pane owns its narrow composition instead of inheriting a toggle: an always-rendered `essentials` region plus a scroll-confined `advanced` disclosure (the OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:47 mobile sequence preview;essentials;advanced/reset/compare), which requires the D-9 API change. W19 deletes the Dock Picker|Blob toggle in the same cut.

### `design` · D-3 · design

**Defect.** No slider in the pane indicates its value: the producer's fill element is correctly sized and paints nothing, while the inert track is painted from a body-text ink token so it reads as 100% filled.

**Mechanism.** ConfigSliderPane.vue:146 sets variant="spectrum" — the producer's chromatic-rail recipe whose `.slider-range` is transparent by design because a gradient supplies the value read. :198-199 admits it ("the spectrum .slider-range is transparent by recipe so no filled/unfilled split reads either") and ships anyway. :202 then re-inks the information-free track with `--ink-muted`, amplifying the empty channel. Figure and ground invert: the background reads as the filled value, the thumb as a hole punched in it. Diagnosed twice historically and cured neither time (N/audit/lanes2/U-CONTROLS.md:54; N/waves/N.W13.md:28).

**Evidence.**

```
/#/atmosphere 1440x900, Colour Energy 0.760 of 0..1 (scratchpad/chD-csp-probe.mjs): .slider-track w962 h24 backgroundColor oklch(0.4469 0.0039 34.63) backgroundImage none; .slider-range width 731 (=76.0%, exactly the value) backgroundColor rgba(0,0,0,0) backgroundImage none opacity 1; [role=slider] w12 h24 backgroundColor rgba(0,0,0,0). Contrast via 1x1 canvas sRGB round-trip: track vs well 5.82:1 light / 8.30:1 dark — identical to the live readout ink (same token). Visible in shots/safari-desktop-light/blob.png (Body Radius 0.220 and Satellites 3 are identical bars) and shots/safari-mobile-dark/atmosphere.png (near-white slab brighter than the display title).
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/blob at >=1024px; compare the Satellites (3) and Body Radius (0.220) rows — two visually identical bars.

**Proposed cure.** Architectural transposition, net deletion: drop variant="spectrum", consume the producer's default neutral Slider so `.slider-range` paints the filled extent from producer tokens. `--slider-track-bg` (:202) and the `:deep(.configurator-row .font-mono)` re-ink (:204-206) then both delete, because the contrast problem they were invented for stops existing — the fill becomes the >=3:1 object and the track returns to a quiet groove. Fixes D-1, D-4 and D-14 with the same cut.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in `demo/shell/dock/Col` · R2-1 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The send button can never submit typed text: `onInputBlur` wipes the contenteditable back to the canonical colour, and because the UA moves focus on mousedown but fires click on mouseup, `onSubmitColor` always reads the already-wiped DOM and re-submits the stale colour.

**Mechanism.** DOM-as-source-of-truth: a mutable DOM node is both the draft store and the read-back channel, with two handlers writing and reading it in UA-imposed order.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:191 (`inputColorRef.value.innerText = formattedCurrentColor.value`) and :215 (`parseAndSetColor(inputColorRef.value.innerText)`). Live measurement: {"committed":"rgb(255 0 0)","typed":"rebeccapurple","afterBlur":"rgb(255 0 0)","afterSend":"rgb(255 0 0)"} in 292 ms total. Short-circuit that hides it: demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:69.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/ → dock → 'Toggle action bar' → 'Open color input'. Focus the field, type a new valid colour, click the arrow (send) button within 2 s. Nothing happens; ~2 s later the colour changes anyway.

**Proposed cure.** Retire the contenteditable as the source of truth. Hold the draft in a local `shallowRef<string>` written by @input, render from it, and let blur only decide whether to DISCARD the draft — never rewrite a node a pending click is about to read. `onSubmitColor` then reads the ref and the blur/click ordering is irrelevant. This is edict 7's synchronous-local-cache idiom, and it dissolves round 1's C-16 (six imperative innerText writers over three sources of truth) in the same stroke.

---

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in `demo/shell/dock/Col` · R2-2 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The 2000 ms debounced parse is never cancelled by this component — not on blur, Enter, send, mode switch, or unmount (there is no onUnmounted at all) — so an abandoned edit silently overwrites a later, deliberate colour commit, and can mutate the global model after the component is destroyed.

**Mechanism.** A hidden timer owns pending state with no lifetime binding; combined with round 1's C-8 (the debounce is an app-wide singleton), the only `.cancel()` in the application belongs to ColorPicker's lifetime — so this component's pending parse is cancelled by the wrong unmount and by nothing else.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:92 (`debounce(parseAndSetColor, 2000)`); demo/shared/utils.ts:36-41 (`.cancel()` exists); `grep -rn onUnmounted demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue` → no matches; the sibling consumer DOES cancel at demo/picker/ColorPicker.vue:383. Measured with real timers: `C1 committed #abcdef` → `C2 after committing #00ff00 color=rgb(0 255 0)` → `C3 2.3s later, nothing touched color=rgb(255 0 0)`. Unmount path: demo/shell/dock/Dock.vue:153 `v-if="hasAnyActionBar"` and :156 `v-if="actionBar"`.
```

**Reproduction.** npx vite-node --config vite.config.ts <scratchpad>/parseprobe2.ts — scenario C. In the app: type a colour into the dock input, blur without committing, immediately pick a different colour by slider/swatch; ~2 s later the colour reverts to the abandoned text.

**Proposed cure.** Give the pending commit the component's lifetime and make it visible reactive state rather than a hidden timer: fold the debounce into the draft `shallowRef` of R2-1, cancel on every path that resolves the edit, and add `onBeforeUnmount`. Do not merely add a cancel call — the singleton ownership (C-8) is the same defect seen from the other side.

---

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-1 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Focus indicator is the user's raw alpha-bearing colour with the UA outline suppressed; measured contrast 1.00:1 for reachable colours; no forced-colors fallback; error state overwrites focus state.

**Mechanism.** Accessibility-critical state expressed through a user-data-driven decorative channel with no floor, no fallback register, and no separation from a competing state — instead of a design-system focus register.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:16 (`focus-visible:outline-none`) and :162-166 (`if (inputIsFocused.value) return { borderColor: cssColor.value }`). Live after a real click: `focused: true, outline: "none 1px rgb(0,95,204)", inlineStyle: "border-color: lab(92 88.8 20 / 0.827);"`. Measured contrast vs the field's own measured background rgb(251,250,248): `#fbfaf8` → 1.00, `rgb(255 255 255)` → 1.04, `oklch(0.7 0.1 200 / 0)` → alpha 0 / invisible. Forced-colors roster membership measured via el.matches() against foundation.css:702-720: colorInput → false, sendBtn → true; colorInput attrs = [data-v-…, contenteditable, role, aria-label, class] (no tabindex). Error+focus measured inline style: `border-color: var(--destructive)`. Contrast-safe primitive available and unused for this purpose: demo/color-session/useContrastSafeColor.ts:273-292, injected at ColorInput.vue:157 and spent on the arrow stroke at :74/:81.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/ at >=1440px viewport → hover the dock → click `Tools` (aria-label="Toggle action bar") → click `Open color input` → drag the alpha channel slider to 0 (or set the colour to white in light mode) → click the field. Focus is undetectable. In WHCM the element matches no outline-restoration selector.

**Proposed cure.** Delete `focus-visible:outline-none` and the focus branch of `inputStyle` entirely. Focus comes from the house register — demo/styles/focus-ring.css / the `.focus-ring` class that four sibling dock controls measurably carry (measured siblingButtonsUsingFocusRing: 4, colorInput.usesFocusRing: false). Architecturally this dissolves by adopting glass-ui `Input`, which owns its own focus register. If the live colour must appear on this control it appears as a swatch, never as the focus boundary.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D2-01 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The mobile dock action bar is a row of dead buttons: on /#/gradient, /#/generate and /#/mix the Tools trigger opens an action layer whose buttons are named, sized, focusable and clickable, and every handler is a silent no-op on mobile.

**Mechanism.** Desktop-only capability wiring masked by optional chaining. demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:206-208 (gradient), :193-196 (generate), :217-220 (mix) call `paneRefs.<view>.value?.<cmd>?.()`. demo/color-picker/App.vue:320-327 writes gradientPaneRef/generatePaneRef/mixPaneRef ONLY inside onDesktopLeftMount/onDesktopRightMount — the file's own comment at :314-316 says "Populated by the onMount callbacks on the desktop PaneSlots". The mobile PaneSlot (App.vue:81-89) passes no :on-mount. Violates owner edict 2 (no masking fallbacks) at the highest-cost site.

**Evidence.**

```
D-dock-probe6.mjs, clipboard pre-seeded with SENTINEL-NOT-TOUCHED then "Copy CSS" clicked in the dock action bar on /#/gradient:
MOBILE 390x844:   clipboard="SENTINEL-NOT-TOUCHED"                                          pageErrors=[]
DESKTOP 1440x900: clipboard="linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(75% 0.15 145deg) 0.00%, oklch(7…" pageErrors=[]
Bar renders in full at 390 (D-dock-probe5.mjs): slot opacity:1, trigger tabIndex:0 32x32; Back 44x44, Reset 32x32, Copy CSS 32x32, Seed from palette 32x32.
```

**Reproduction.** node D-dock-probe6.mjs against the live dev server: newContext 390x844 → goto /#/gradient → seed clipboard → click "Toggle action bar" → click the button named exactly "Copy CSS" → read clipboard. Repeat at 1440x900.

**Proposed cure.** Invert the direction of the dependency: the dock must not hold references to pane instances. The pane provide()s a command object keyed by view; the dock injects it. A view that provides no commands renders no Tools trigger; a provided command is live in whichever slot mounted it, by construction. Delete paneRefs, onDesktopLeftMount, onDesktopRightMount and every `?.` on a handler — an action that can be absent must be ABSENT, never present-and-inert.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D2-02 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The Picker's entire action region does not exist on a phone: Copy color, Reset color, Random color, Palettes, Extract palette and Open color input are in the dock on desktop and absent from the whole document on mobile.

Same probe at 1440x900: all four true. D-dock-probe3.mjs·C at 390: slot gridTemplateColumns "0px", opacity "0", tabIndex -1.

**Proposed cure.** Same inversion as D2-01. The Picker publishes its command set through provide(); the dock injects. Deletes the `?? null` mask, the desktop-only ref, and the whole desktop/mobile capability fork in App.vue at once.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D2-03 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Two contradictory isDesktop predicates (plus two more Tailwind/media forks) — at 1024x1366 portrait the dock renders the MOBILE pane switcher and the DESKTOP profile block simultaneously, with no ⋮ menu and no Tools.

**Mechanism.** demo/styles/shell.css:98-112 documents that the data-layout stamp exists so the two witnesses "can never disagree" and warns that a media wrapper "would re-introduce the width-only disagreement the stamp exists to retire" — Dock.vue:71 re-introduces exactly that predicate. Predicates: App.vue:310-312 `(min-width:1024px) and (min-aspect-ratio:1.1)`; Dock.vue:71 `(min-width:1024px)` width-only; ProfileSection.vue:48 / MobileMenuDropdown.vue:38 Tailwind `lg:` width-only; PaneSegmentedControl.vue:46 `@media (max-width:639px)`.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (visual truth, state coverage, motion, ` · D-04 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The failed state carries no programmatic semantics at all and evaporates after 2 s while the invalid text remains.

```

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The failure-explicit Result contract is destroyed at the demo boundary, so a LIBRARY CRASH and a USER TYPO are the same event. src/css/grammar.ts:181 throws TypeError on eight inputs; demo/color-session/picker-color.ts:109-113 is a throwing facade that only handles `result.ok === false` and lets the raw TypeError escape past its own typed PickerColorError; demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:80-85 catches everything with a bare `catch {}` and renders the hardcoded literal 'not a valid color'. Re-derived and confirmed this pass; first filed by the prior pass.

**Mechanism.** Contract transposition, three hops, each lossy: Result → exception (picker-color.ts valueOrThrow/PickerColorError) → bare catch {} (useColorParsing) → a constant string literal (ColorInput.vue:88). The discriminant is erased at every hop. Two further silencers compound it: `initialParse` (useColorParsing.ts:83) suppresses a composable's first failure, and `previousInvalid` (useColorParsing.ts:62) makes any REPEAT of the same string a total no-op — the state a frustrated user reaches within two seconds.

**Evidence.**

```
Contract: src/css/types.ts:25-27 (`ParseResult<T> = {ok:true,...} | {ok:false, diagnostics:[ParseIssue,...]}`), package.json:4 ('failure-explicit'). Throw site: src/css/grammar.ts:181 `splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g," "), "space")` where splitTopLevel("","/") returns [] (grammar.ts:79,85). Measured against the exact dist the dev server serves (dist/subpaths/css.js, Jul 24 14:04, newer than src/css/grammar.ts Jul 17 14:48): all of oklch() rgb() hsl() lab() lch() color() oklab() 'hsl(  )' THROW `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')`, while 'oklch(' and 'notacolor' correctly return ok:false. Live at http://localhost:9000/#/ after typing oklch()+Enter: {"inputText":"oklch()","errorBadge":null,"borderColor":"lab(92 88.8 20 / 0.827)"} and browser_console_messages(level=error) → Errors: 0. Diagnostics loss is total: ParseIssue carries {code,start,end,expected,actual} (src/css/types.ts:10-24) and the only three demo readers of `.diagnostics` are internal-invariant sites with no human present (useGradientCSS.ts:128, useImageSampler.ts:60, useMarkdownColors.ts:33); the one surface where a human types CSS reads it never.
```

**Reproduction.** `npx vitest run --config docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/shell-dock-colorinput/probe.vitest.config.ts` → 5/5 green. The harness drives the REAL useColorParsing composable and the REAL picker-color facade against the PUBLISHED dist/subpaths/{color,css}.js and proves: (1) all eight throw while 'oklch(' returns ok:false — the crash is narrower than the failure path and therefore invisible beside it; (2) parsePickerColor re-throws a raw TypeError, not PickerColorError; (3) with the initialParse gate burned, all eight leave the model object-identical (toBe) and set the same boolean a typo sets; (4) the first parse of a composable's life leaves parseError===false; (5) a repeat of the same crasher early-returns on previousInvalid — no badge, no console, no model change. Live: the gesture chain above.

**Proposed cure.** Delete valueOrThrow and PickerColorError from picker-color.ts and every bare catch on the parse path. parseCssColor already returns the right shape — let it reach the component: `parsePickerColor(s): ParseResult<CssColor>`; `useColorParsing.commit(source)` returns it; ColorInput renders `diagnostics[0].expected` and marks [start,end] in the field. No try, no catch, no previousInvalid cache. An unexpected TypeError then has nowhere to hide — it becomes a page error the existing visual-audit harness already reports, so 'pageErrors — 0' becomes a real green instead of a manufactured one.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 (re-derived, confirms prior pass) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** parseCssColor is typed and documented as total and failure-explicit (ParseResult<CssColor>; package.json:4 says "failure-explicit") but is partial: it throws TypeError on eight degenerate functional bodies. demo/color-session/picker-color.ts:104-113 then inverts the library's entire Result surface back into exceptions (valueOrThrow / PickerColorError), and demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:84 catches with a BARE `catch {}` that cannot distinguish a user typo from a library crash and discards the library's structured ParseIssue[] diagnostics entirely. ColorInput.vue SWALLOWS MT-F001 — it neither guards nor crashes.

**Mechanism.** error-model inversion at a library boundary whose declared totality is untrue (Mechanism A)

**Evidence.**

```
src/css/grammar.ts:181 `splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g," "),"space")` — the `!` is false. Against shipped dist: `node -e "const {parseCssColor}=await import('./dist/subpaths/css.js')..."` → oklch() rgb() hsl() lab() lch() color() oklab() 'hsl(  )' all THREW `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')`; oklch(0.7 0.1 200) => ok:true. Swallow site: demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:84 (bare catch, no binding). Diagnostics discarded: picker-color.ts:112 captures result.diagnostics into PickerColorError; useColorParsing.ts:84 drops the whole error object.
```

**Reproduction.** Live at http://localhost:9000/#/ (Playwright): open dock action bar → click 'Open color input' → set the contenteditable to 'oklch(' then 'oklch()' + dispatch input; wait past the 2000 ms debounce; then dispatch keydown Enter. Measured result: {"afterDebounce":{"text":"oklch()","badge":"not a valid color","errClass":true},"afterEnter":{same},"errs":[]}. THE USER SEES: a red --destructive border plus the badge 'not a valid color' — identical to typing 'notacolor'. Zero page errors, zero console errors, nothing records that the library threw.

**Proposed cure.** Fix grammar.ts:181 to return failure(source) on an empty body so parseCssColor is total AS DECLARED, then DELETE valueOrThrow and PickerColorError outright and let Result cross the demo boundary unconverted. useColorParsing.parseAndSetColor becomes a branch on result.ok and the error badge renders result.diagnostics. Net deletion: 12 catch blocks and the constant 'not a valid color' string; MT-F001 becomes an unrepresentable state rather than a masked one.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The library's sole failure protocol is destroyed at the demo boundary, and the live MT-F001 TypeError is swallowed indistinguishably from a user typo. picker-color.ts re-throws the Result as an exception; useColorParsing catches it bare; ColorInput renders one flat badge.

**Mechanism.** A — the failure-explicit contract is inverted at the demo boundary: Result → throw → bare un-narrowed catch → one flat English string. Once re-thrown, the catch cannot discriminate a declared parse failure from a library bug.

**Evidence.**

```
src/css/grammar.ts:181 (`splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g," "), "space")` — the `!` is false for an empty body); demo/color-session/picker-color.ts:104-113 (valueOrThrow / parsePickerColor throw); demo/color-session/useColorParsing.ts:88-95 (`} catch { previousInvalid = input; if (!initialParse) flashParseError(); }`); demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:87-89. `grep -n "instanceof PickerColorError" demo` → no matches. Authority: docs/tranches/V/ARCHITECTURE.md:245 — "ParseResult<T> = … is the only parse-failure protocol … never a partial Color or substitute." Live probe output: AFTER ENTER {"text":"oklch()","errorBadgeText":"not a valid color","errorClass":true,"newConsoleErrors":[],"newPageErrors":[]} vs CONTROL zzzzz {"errorBadgeText":"not a valid color","errorClass":true,"newConsoleErrors":[]} — bit-identical. In-graph library probe: "oklch()" -> THROWS TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace'); "zzzzz" -> ok:false + diagnostics.
```

**Reproduction.** Dev server at http://localhost:9000. Fresh Chromium context, 1440x900. Navigate to /#/, wait 2.5s. Click button[aria-label="Toggle action bar"], then button[aria-label="Open color input"]. Click span.color-input, Meta+A, type `oklch(` then `)`, press Enter. Observe: red badge "not a valid color", zero console errors, zero page errors. Repeat with `zzzzz` — identical. Script: /private/tmp/claude-504/-Users-mkbabb-Programming-value-js/6614e90c-8bd6-434f-b017-5ad4277c6e5e/scratchpad/CHL-colorinput-probe.mjs. Screenshot: .../scratchpad/CHL-oklch.png. Node-only: `node --input-type=module -e "import {parseCssColor} from './dist/subpaths/css.js'; parseCssColor('oklch()')"` → TypeError.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the exception channel entirely. `parsePickerColor` returns ParseResult<CssColor> unconverted; useColorParsing consumes it structurally with NO try/catch, so a genuine exception propagates to demo/color-picker/ErrorBoundary.vue where it is visible; `parseError` becomes ShallowRef<readonly ParseIssue[] | null> and ColorInput renders diagnostics[0].code/.expected — the library already carries UTF-16 spans, expected, actual and stable codes (src/css/types.ts:10-23) and the demo throws all of them away. valueOrThrow survives only for total operations. Separately, at the library: src/css/grammar.ts:181 `slash[0] ?? ""` so the empty-body path falls out as components.length !== 3 → failure(source). Net deletion, no new abstraction.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-30 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** A failed login is completely silent: the slug-edit layer closes as if it succeeded, and there are three independent, redundant reasons no error can reach the user. (1) `SlugEditLayer.vue:54` calls `pm.onSlugSwitch(...)` with no `await` inside a `try`, while `useSlugMigration.ts:51` declares it `async` — the `catch` at :57 is unreachable, and :55-56 close the layer synchronously before the network call resolves. (2) It would not matter: the callee catches everything itself (`useSlugMigration.ts:74-88`) and dispatches onto `slugBarRef.value?.setError(...)`, a permanently-null ref. (3) `slugError` is written at six sites in SlugEditLayer and rendered at zero — the template contains no reference to it. And the substring branches at :59-62 are a verbatim resurrection of the exact bug `useSlugMigration.ts:77-82` records as killed at S.W2 W2-6 (`ApiProblem` does `super(title)`, so `e.message` is the server title and can never contain '409'/'404'/'429').

**Mechanism.** error channel swallowed three ways: un-awaited async across a component boundary; callee catches into a null instance ref; error state with no template binding — plus a condemned string-sniff re-authored after its cure was recorded

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/layers/SlugEditLayer.vue:54 (un-awaited) · :57 (catch) · :59-62 (substring sniff) · :13,18,43,49,59-62 (slugError writes) · `sed -n '74,119p' SlugEditLayer.vue | grep slugError` → no output (never rendered). demo/palettes/useSlugMigration.ts:51 (`async function onSlugSwitch`) · :77-82 (the S.W2 W2-6 ruling comment) · :84-87 (correct typed path, dispatched into null). demo/platform/transport/api-problem.ts:22-33 (`super(title)`). LIVE REPRODUCTION (LDOCK-probe6.mjs, WebKit 1440x900, http://localhost:9000): after filling 'aaaa-bbbb-cccc-dddd' and clicking button[aria-label="Switch to slug"] — `[after submit] inputValue:"" layerInert:true layerAriaHidden:"true" errorish:[]`, dock still shows 'Login', console tail contains only the two boot lines, no error from the login attempt.
```

**Reproduction.** node LDOCK-probe6.mjs (scratchpad) against the live dev server at http://localhost:9000 — open the dock Login button, fill the slug input, submit; observe layer closes inert+aria-hidden, input cleared, zero error text anywhere in the DOM (`errorish: []`), clean console. The dev server's API is deliberately misconfigured (its own status lamp reads 'dev misconfigured'), so the attempt is guaranteed to fail.

**Proposed cure.** `onSlugSwitch` returns its outcome (`Promise<Result<void, ApiProblem>>`) instead of dispatching it onto a component instance; the layer awaits and renders the failure. One contract change removes all three swallows at once — awaiting makes it observable, returning kills the instance-ref channel, rendering kills the dead state. Rides L-6/L-24's move of the session port to `demo/platform/auth/`, so the login surface becomes a function with a typed result rather than a feature-owned side effect.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The dock view-select trigger renders an EMPTY label on 7 of the demo's 14 views: the Select's model value and its option set are owned by different modules with different ranges, so the selected value routinely matches no SelectItem.

**Mechanism.** split enumeration / no single owner of the navigation taxonomy — the option set and the model value are computed from two different modules

**Evidence.**

```
Screenshots, same matrix (safari-desktop-light, 1440 CSS px, isDesktop true): docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/browse.png shows the trigger reading "Browse"; atmosphere.png and admin-users.png show icon+chevron with NO text. Mechanism from source: DockViewSelect.vue:73 renders `<SelectValue v-if="isDesktop" />` with no placeholder; node_modules/reka-ui/dist/Select/SelectValue.js:33-42 computes `selectedLabel` by looking modelValue up in `rootContext.optionsSet` and falling back to "", and `slotText` returns `props.placeholder` (default "", lines 10-14) when the lookup misses. Ranges: `:model-value="currentView"` (Dock.vue:169) has range 14 (viewSchema.ts:30-44); `viewEntries` (Dock.vue:173) has range 7 at a time (useDockAdminMode.ts:26-27,34-40). All three shots also show the "Login" control, i.e. unauthenticated — capture.mjs has no login step (grep for admin|login|auth returns only the five route literals at lines 32-36).
```

**Reproduction.** Open http://localhost:9000/#/atmosphere (or /#/blob or any /#/admin/*) unauthenticated at viewport width >= 1024px. The dock view-select trigger shows its icon and chevron with no visible label. Compare /#/browse, which reads "Browse". Already captured in the harness shots listed above.

**Proposed cure.** Do NOT add a placeholder — that hides it. Make it structurally impossible: derive the option set as `viewsInGroup(activeGroup) ∪ {currentView}` so the model value is an option by construction. Requires the single nav schema of L-2.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Three hand-maintained enumerations of the same 14 view identifiers exist in three directories, and two of them directly contradict each other about what "admin" means.

**Mechanism.** incomplete SSOT — VIEW_MAP lacks the `group` field the dock needs, so the enumeration regrew in a third home

**Evidence.**

```
(1) demo/shell/viewSchema.ts:30-44 + VIEW_MAP :97-234 — the declared SSOT, 14 ViewIds, and it carries NO grouping field at all. (2) demo/color-picker/router/index.ts:22-37 — 14 routes hand-listed, `meta:{admin:true}` on exactly 5 (:31-35); /atmosphere (:29) and /blob (:30) have no meta. (3) demo/shell/dock/composables/useDockAdminMode.ts:26-27 — `userViews` (7) and `adminViews` (7), where adminViews INCLUDES "atmosphere" and "blob". Router says 5 admin views; the dock says 7. viewSchema.ts's own header claims it exists 'to retire the 4-copy ViewId enumeration'; its accentHueShift comment (:66-79) speaks of 'the nine primary views ... in dock order', a nine the dock has never had.
```

**Reproduction.** grep -n "meta: { admin: true }" demo/color-picker/router/index.ts → 5 hits; sed -n '27p' demo/shell/dock/composables/useDockAdminMode.ts → 7 ids including atmosphere and blob. Adding a view today requires three synchronised edits and nothing fails if you make two.

**Proposed cure.** One home: `demo/shell/nav/nav-schema.ts` — VIEW_MAP gains `group: "primary"|"tuning"|"admin"` and `path`; `routes.ts` DERIVES the route table from it (`Object.entries(VIEW_MAP).map(...)`) so a view cannot exist without a route; the dock reads `viewsInGroup(g)` instead of re-listing ids.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D1 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The five palette swatches are inert, AT-invisible spans: `tag="button"`, `@click="copyColor(css)"` and `:aria-label` are all dropped by glass-ui 7.0.0's WatercolorDot (no `tag` prop; `inheritAttrs:false`; only class/style forwarded; hardcoded `<span aria-hidden="true">` with inline `pointer-events:none`). copyColor() is unreachable dead code while `cursor-pointer`/`active:scale-95` still advertise the affordance.

**Mechanism.** glass-7 API break unmigrated by the W44 adoption commit f2c8f565; `inheritAttrs:false` silently swallows consumer listeners and ARIA — a class of failure Vue's type system cannot surface.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:199-208 vs node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/watercolor-dot/WatercolorDot.vue.d.ts (props: color|variant|animate|cycleDuration|range|seed — no `tag`) and dist/watercolor-dot.js (`inheritAttrs: !1`, `o("span", { "aria-hidden": "true", … pointerEvents: "none" })`). Live probe at http://localhost:9000/#/generate returned {"tag":"SPAN","ariaHidden":"true","ariaLabel":null,"tagAttr":null,"pointerEvents":"none","tabIndex":-1,"vueListeners":[]}; sw.focus() then document.activeElement===sw === false. PaletteColorStrip.vue:3-4 is also aria-hidden/role=presentation.
```

**Reproduction.** Load http://localhost:9000/#/generate; evaluate [...document.querySelectorAll('[data-generate-plate] .generate-swatch')].map(el=>({tag:el.tagName,ariaHidden:el.getAttribute('aria-hidden'),pointerEvents:getComputedStyle(el).pointerEvents,vueListeners:Object.keys(el._vei||{})})) — all five are SPAN / "true" / "none" / []. Clicking a swatch copies nothing.

**Proposed cure.** Transpose the seat, don't patch the prop: the glass-7 dot is a FACE, not a SEAT. Wrap it in a real `<button type="button" :aria-label="`Copy ${css}`">` that owns the click, the accessible name and the focus ring, with `<WatercolorDot>` as its already-aria-hidden child. Restores verb, name and keyboard operability in one move with no per-instance styling. Same fix retires the identical dead `tag="button"` seats at MixSourceSelector.vue:168,215 and CurrentPaletteEditor.vue:98. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

**Defect.** The component's ONLY automated oracle is red and CI never runs it. o20-generate-plate.spec.ts test 2 fails on an irreconcilable serialization mismatch (raw `oklch(83.35…% … 333.86…deg)` stamped vs computed `oklch(0.833541 0.0926939 333.865)`), and no GitHub workflow invokes `test:e2e`. The surviving test 1 is vacuous.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · C1 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Dragging (or shift+arrow nudging) a handle past a neighbour leaves `stops` non-monotonic: the rail's ramp positions descend so CSS clamps them into a flat band, the easing intervals now straddle inverted pairs, and the serialized CSS is refused by the app's own parser — the atomic round-trip contract is one-way only.

**Mechanism.** Sortedness is enforced on insert and merely coincidental on update — the position write is a raw array map, so an invariant the rest of the system depends on has no owner.

**Evidence.**

```
Live: after adding a stop at 50% and dragging the 0% handle right, DOM/model order = ["Gradient stop at 75%","Gradient stop at 50%","Gradient stop at 100%"]; editor text = `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(0.75 0.15 145) 74.9%, oklch(70% 0.165 205deg) 50%, oklch(0.65 0.18 265) 100%)`; rail background-image head = `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(0.75 0.15 145) 74.9%, oklch(0.746875 0.150937 148.75) 73.34%, oklch(0.74375 0.151875 152.5) 71.79%, …)` (descending). `npx vite-node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/parse-probe.ts` → `parseGradientCSS -> reject: stop positions must be non-decreasing (hard-stop reordering isn't modeled)` on that exact string. Code: demo/workbenches/gradient/composables/useGradientModel.ts:127-131 (updateStop never sorts) vs :118 (addStop does); emit sites GradientStopEditor.vue:145 and :179; guard-on-input at demo/workbenches/gradient/composables/gradientParse.ts:284-290.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000 → dock → Gradient → click the rail at 50% → drag the 0% handle to ~75% → read the CSS box and the rail's computed background-image; paste that CSS back into the editor and the app rejects it.

**Proposed cure.** Make ordering a property of the operation, not of the array: one model action `setStopPosition(id, pos)` that maps then sorts, so sortedness is total by construction, paired with C2's identity-keyed intervals so the re-sort cannot scramble easing. Clamping the drag to (prev, next) is cheaper and strictly worse — it makes stops un-reorderable.

---

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · C2 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Easing intervals are keyed by array INDEX and synced by a length watcher that appends/truncates at the TAIL, while addStop inserts in sorted position — so inserting a stop silently moves the user's authored curve onto a different pair of stops and reverts the authored segment to linear.

**Mechanism.** An adjacency relation (which pair of stops a curve belongs to) is modelled as a positional array, and the sync watcher observes only `stops.length`, so it cannot know where the stop landed and always guesses 'at the end'.

**Evidence.**

```
Live: stops [0,50,100] with `steps(4, jump-end)` authored on segment 50→100 → readouts ["cubic-bezier(0, 0, 1, 1)","steps(4, jump-end)"], heads ["1 → 2linear","2 → 3steps"]. After clicking the rail at 25%: labels [0%,25%,50%,100%], readouts ["cubic-bezier(0, 0, 1, 1)","steps(4, jump-end)","cubic-bezier(0, 0, 1, 1)"], heads ["1 → 2linear","2 → 3steps","3 → 4linear"] — steps now governs 25→50; the authored 50→100 curve is gone. Code: demo/workbenches/gradient/composables/useGradientModel.ts:89-100.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000 → Gradient → click the rail at 50% → open interval row 2 → click the `steps` specimen → click the rail at 25% → read `.readout-rail code` and `.interval-head` text.

**Proposed cure.** Hang the interval on the stop that OPENS it — `GradientStop { id, cssColor, position, easing }` (last stop's easing ignored) — so the curve travels with its own stop through any insert/remove/sort. That deletes the parallel array, the sync watcher, and all three `Gradient interval N is missing` throws (useGradientCSS.ts:189/240, GradientVisualizer.vue:77). Alternative: a Map keyed by the ordered stop-id pair.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/workbenches/gen` · C-1 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The per-swatch copy verb is entirely dead: `tag="button"`, `:aria-label` and `@click` are all silently discarded by glass-ui 7.0.0's WatercolorDot, which renders a hardcoded aria-hidden span with pointer-events:none. The swatches are not buttons, have no accessible name, are not focusable, and cannot be clicked; copyColor() is unreachable.

**Mechanism.** Producer/consumer contract break absorbed by Vue's legal-fallthrough semantics: a component with inheritAttrs:false that hand-forwards a whitelist of attrs silently swallows every unlisted attr AND every listener, and the type system treats unknown attributes on a component as valid fallthrough. The consumer's documented idiom (tag="button") was never a prop.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:199-208 (the four bindings) · node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/watercolor-dot/WatercolorDot.vue.d.ts:23-52 declares {color,variant?,animate?,cycleDuration?,range?,seed?} and no emits · `grep -o "tag" node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/watercolor-dot.js | wc -l` → 0 · watercolor-dot.js setup body: `inheritAttrs:!1` with `n=h(), c=i(()=>n.class), f=i(()=>n.style)` (class/style only) and root `<span aria-hidden="true" style=[attrs, {backgroundColor, borderRadius, pointerEvents:"none", …}]>` · LIVE DOM at http://localhost:9000/#/generate: {"tag":"SPAN","ariaHidden":"true","ariaLabel":null,"tabIndex":-1,"tagAttr":null,"pointerEvents":"none","cursor":"pointer","w":40,"h":40} and tabbablesInPlate = [INPUT "Palette name", BUTTON "Regenerate", BUTTON "Save palette", BUTTON "Copy all colors"] — the 5 swatches contribute 0
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/generate → click any colour swatch inside [data-generate-plate] → nothing is copied, no focus moves, no state changes. Structurally: the DOM dump above (SPAN / aria-hidden=true / pointer-events:none / tabIndex -1 / aria-label null).

**Proposed cure.** The dot is a FACE, not a SEAT. Transpose rather than patch: a real <button> seat wraps the dot (dot stays the decorative aria-hidden child it was built to be; the button carries name, focus affordance, ≥24px target, listener). Better for a 9-consumer species: glass-ui grows the seat — a WatercolorDotButton, or an explicit as/tag + attr-forwarding contract on WatercolorDot. Relay R-1 to the glass BH inbox; the consumer half is BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8. Do NOT delete the three dead bindings — that retires the verb, not the defect.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-1 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Per-swatch copy verb is inert: `tag="button"` is not a prop in glass-ui 7.0.0. The producer renders a `<span aria-hidden="true">` with inline `pointer-events:none`, so the click cannot land, the `aria-label` is discarded, and the element is not focusable. Directly re-litigates the P051 abrogation that names Generate as an execute site.

**Mechanism.** F1 — producer contract drift: glass-ui 7.0.0 landed the P051 removal while the consumer still writes the 6.x interactive-host idiom; Vue discards the unknown prop silently, so no typecheck, console warning or unit test fires.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:199-208 passes `tag="button"` + `:aria-label` + `@click`. Producer prop set is `{color,variant?,animate?,cycleDuration?,range?,seed?}` (node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/watercolor-dot/WatercolorDot.vue.d.ts) — no `tag`. Render fn (dist/watercolor-dot.js): `o("span", {"aria-hidden":"true", ... style: u([f.value,{...pointerEvents:"none"...}])})`. Live measure on /#/generate: `{tagName:"SPAN", ariaHidden:"true", ariaLabel:null, tagAttr:null, role:null, tabIndex:-1, pointerEvents:"none", cursor:"pointer", rect:{w:40,h:40}}`; `hitTestAtDotCentre:{tag:"DIV", cls:"px-3 pb-1 flex flex-wrap gap-1.5", isTheDot:false}`; `swatchRowFocusable:0`. `grep -rn "generate-swatch" demo/ src/` returns only GenerateControls.vue:205, foundation.css:689, foundation.css:834 — no pointer-events override exists. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:91 and OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:108 both abrogate the `tag="button"` host and name Generate an execute site.
```

**Reproduction.** Dev server live at localhost:9000. Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate, then in the page: `const d=document.querySelector('.generate-swatch'); const r=d.getBoundingClientRect(); document.elementFromPoint(r.x+r.width/2, r.y+r.height/2)` → returns the parent `DIV.px-3.pb-1.flex.flex-wrap.gap-1.5`, not the dot. `d.tagName` → "SPAN"; `d.getAttribute('aria-label')` → null; `d.tabIndex` → -1; `getComputedStyle(d).pointerEvents` → "none". Clicking any swatch in the UI copies nothing.

**Proposed cure.** Architectural, per the canon's own prescription (OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:108): the dot becomes a noninteractive face inside a NAMED GEOMETRIC SEAT — a native `<button type="button">` at >=44px carrying the accessible name, the focus ring and the click, with the untouched organic dot as its child. Structurally identical to the Gradient stop treatment already specified at VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:206. This single transposition restores the copy verb, the accessible name, the keyboard path and the tap floor at once. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (RC-1, RC-2, RC-3).

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-2 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The editable plate title is a false affordance: the typed palette name is emitted, then discarded at the persistence boundary. The consumer's handler signature has one parameter and hard-codes the literal "Generated Palette".

**Mechanism.** F2 — the emit that ends nowhere: a value is produced, typed, named and emitted at every layer except the one that persists it. The in-file comment at :44-47 records the gap as 'its owner's one-liner' and asserts 'this emit is already truthful' — provenance prose defending a wire that was never connected.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:48-52 declares `save: [colors: string[], name: string]`; :102-104 emits `paletteName.value`. GeneratePane.vue:14-20: `function onSave(colors: string[]) { ... pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors); }` — the `name` arg is dropped. Live reproduction result: `{inputValueAfterType:"ZZ-CHALLENGE-D-NAME", libraryMentionsTypedName:false, libraryMentionsDefault:true, storedNames:["\"name\":\"Generated Palette\""], liveRegions:[]}`. Compounding: measured at-rest affordance on the input is `{border:"0px solid", textDecoration:"none", background:"rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)"}` — the dashed underline at :148 is `hover:` only, so on touch the control is permanently undiscoverable (PROPORTION-AUDIT.md:51 PR-07).
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. Set the plate title input to `ZZ-CHALLENGE-D-NAME` (native value setter + dispatch `input`), click the `Save palette` button, wait 700ms, then read `localStorage['color-palettes']`. The persisted entry is `"name":"Generated Palette"`. The typed string appears neither in the DOM nor in storage, and no status/alert/aria-live region is emitted.

**Proposed cure.** Gestalt, not the one-line signature patch: a rename affordance with no visible boundary, no label and no confirmation does not belong on the specimen plate. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:102 already homes it — 'rename/lifecycle/export actions and durable operation state live in the selected inspector.' Delete the plate field; the Library inspector owns rename. That is also the KISS cure and costs the user nothing. If the field is retained instead, `onSave(colors, name)` must carry the name AND gain a visible at-rest boundary plus a durable save confirmation. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (same hold covers GeneratePane.vue).

**Defect.** The generated palette — the workbench's entire output — is wholly absent from the accessibility tree. Both renderings of the specimen are aria-hidden, so a screen-reader user receives zero colour information.

The full AT transcript for the region is: a text field, the numeral 5, three verbs, and `seed: 71f806ff`. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:83 — 'Selected, failed, pending, withdrawn and disabled states are never color-only.'

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-1 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The per-swatch Copy verb cannot be triggered by any input path. `tag="button"` is not a prop in glass-ui 7.0.0 (P051 removed the interactive host), so WatercolorDot renders a `<span aria-hidden="true">` with no role, no tabindex, the authored `:aria-label` silently dropped, and `pointer-events: none`. copyColor() and its @click are unreachable code, while the consumer's own `cursor: pointer` advertises a clickable target that cannot be clicked.

**Mechanism.** A — dead producer contract: authored against an interactive host Glass 7 deleted; Vue fallthrough $attrs make the removal structurally un-typecheckable, so HARD demo typecheck stays green.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:199-208 (tag="button", :aria-label, @click). Producer d.ts node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/watercolor-dot/*.d.ts declares only {color, variant, animate, cycleDuration, range, seed} — no `tag`. Rendered (identical WebKit+Chromium): {"tag":"SPAN","role":null,"tabindex":null,"ariaHidden":"true","ariaLabel":null,"focusable":false}. Hit-test: {"pointerEvents":"none","cursor":"pointer","elementAtCenter":"DIV.px-3.pb-1","centerIsSwatchOrChild":false}. Real click: `CLICK ERROR: page.click: Timeout 30000ms exceeded.` / `clipboard after clicking swatch: "SENTINEL-BEFORE"` / `=> POINTER COPY WORKS: false`. Chromium tab order from the name input: ["BUTTON[Regenerate]","BUTTON[Save palette]","BUTTON[Copy all colors]","BUTTON[Generation preset]","BUTTON[Color harmony]","SPAN[Color count]"] — no swatch. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:91 abrogates tag="button" and names Generate as an execution site; the component's own comment :194-196 still asserts the abrogated law.
```

**Reproduction.** node gc-probe6.mjs (hit-test + real click + clipboard sentinel); node gc-probe3.mjs (a11y + Chromium tab order). Deterministic, no timing dependence.

**Proposed cure.** Execute P051 at the Generate site as the constitution writes it: WatercolorDot is an ornamental face; a named geometric <button> seat encloses it and owns activation, accessible name and focus. Wave-level transposition, not a local shim or an aria sprinkle.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-2 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The specimen has no non-visual existence: every colour-bearing element in the plate is aria-hidden, so a screen-reader user receives the count, three verbs and the seed — and no colour information whatsoever, which is the workbench's entire output.

Chromium AT walk halting at each aria-hidden boundary: [{HIDDEN_SUBTREE: DIV.overflow-hidden (PaletteColorStrip)}, {ctrl INPUT 'Palette name'}, {text '5'}, {ctrl BUTTON 'Regenerate'}, {ctrl BUTTON 'Save palette'}, {ctrl BUTTON 'Copy all colors'}, {HIDDEN_SUBTREE: SPAN.generate-swatch} x5, {text 'seed: 70b7c345'}]. demo/palettes/browser/card/PaletteColorStrip.vue:2-4 is aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" BY CONTRACT ('W5-a11y: color strip is a decorative visual, hidden from AT'). ### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-3 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The count ramp interpolates in sRGB, destroying 59.9–63.7% of the chroma at every segment midpoint — in a colour library, on the instrument whose stated job is to show the palette's own state. Stops are authored in OKLCh; no interpolation hint is given.

**Mechanism.** B — sRGB in a colour lab: hand-rolled CSS gradient with OKLCh stops and no `in oklch` hint; colour correctness was never given an owner in this component.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:65-73 `return \`linear-gradient(to right, ${stops.join(", ")})\``. Measured (Chromium): hasInterpolationHint:false. Segment 0->1 C(a)=0.1831 C(b)=0.1617 C_painted_mid=0.0626 vs C_true_oklch_mid=0.1724 => 63.7% loss; 1->2 62.9%; 2->3 62.4%; 3->4 59.9%. Only 5 of 434px sit on a stop, so most of the ramp's area is sRGB mud. Visible in docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-mobile-light/generate.png: the strip's bands are saturated while the ramp 40px below passes through grey-brown. Component comment :62-64 claims 'the instrument shows its own state'. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:143 'No path desaturates through gray'; §1 'value.js is a chromatic laboratory'.
```

**Reproduction.** node gc-probe4.mjs. Seed-independent — the loss is a property of the interpolation, not the palette.

**Proposed cure.** The ramp stops being a hand-rolled CSS gradient: either the producer Slider carries a colour-bearing track (the VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:104 'colour-bearing or neutral track chosen by semantics' arm), or the gradient is generated by this repo's own interpolation. A colour library emitting an un-hinted sRGB gradient is a contradiction in terms.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-3 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** On touch, tapping inside a SELECTED stop handle's own advertised hit region DELETES the stop. The remove chip (top-11, 44px below the rail) carries the same always-on hit inflation (--touch-target 2.75rem = 44px) as the handle, and the selected handle's scale(1.25) also scales its ::before expander to 55px, so the two invisible regions overlap with the destructive one on top (chip z-20 vs handle z-10). No undo, no confirmation, no visual cue.

**Mechanism.** two invisible expanded hit targets stacked on one axis, with the destructive one painted on top

**Evidence.**

```
iPhone 14 / WebKit, evidence/p4.mjs T11: `T11 before {"n":3,"pos":["0","50","100"]}` → select mid stop → `T11 after tap at handle-centre + 20px {"n":2,"pos":["0","100"]}`. Hit map from p3.mjs T7: `handle-hit offsets -26 → 13 | chip-hit offsets 14 → 40` (offsets from handle centre); `expander 44px x 44px`. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:288 (top-11, z-20), :262 (scale 1.25), :374-391 (::before expander), :385-391 (coarse 44px).
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p4.mjs — block T11 (iPhone 14 context against http://localhost:9000/#/gradient)

**Proposed cure.** The remove verb leaves the rail entirely and lives in the selected-stop inspector, where VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5 already puts lifecycle actions ('Full detail, rename/lifecycle/export actions and durable operation state live in the selected inspector'). This is the same transposition that cures D-4 and D-9. If a rail-local remove must survive, it goes on the opposite side of the rail from the drag axis and the two expanders are made mutually exclusive.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-2 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** No ordering invariant: a drag can carry a stop across its neighbour, leaving stops[] non-monotonic. sampleCoalescedStops then emits DESCENDING percentages into a CSS linear-gradient, which the browser clamps into a hard band, and the per-interval easing curves silently re-pair because intervals are keyed by array index rather than by the stop pair.

**Mechanism.** an ordinal identity that exists in the UI vocabulary ('stop 2', '1 → 2') but has no representation the model defends

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p4.mjs T10: `T10 stops after cross {"n":4,"pos":["0","29","8","100"],"centers":[235,362.6,270.6,675]}`; painted rail percentages `[…,26.36, 29, 27.1, 25.2, 23.3, 21.4, 19.5, 17.6, 15.7, 13.8,…]` with `descendingPairs=[[12,29,27.1],…,[21,11.9,10]]`. Screenshot evidence/D02-cross-drag-hard-band.png shows the resulting seam. Source: useGradientModel.ts:127-131 (updateStop never sorts; addStop at :118 does), useGradientCSS.ts:195-200 (pos = s0.position + t*(s1-s0), negative range), GradientVisualizer.vue:64-88 (colorAtPosition assumes sorted). Interval labels stay index-keyed: p2.log T3 `["1 → 2","2 → 3"]`.
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p4.mjs — block T10

**Proposed cure.** The model owns the ordinal: re-sort on every position commit and re-key intervals to the stop PAIR, or clamp a dragged stop into (prev, next). Only then is VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5.2's explicit reorder grammar (Space grabs, 'position of total' announcement, Escape cancels) implementable at all.

---

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-1 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The instrument has two different position axes for one domain quantity. The ramp is painted across the full border-box; handles, the add-ghost and the pointer inverse ride an inset [HANDLE_HALF, W-HANDLE_HALF] track. They agree only at 50%. The file's own doc-comment asserts they 'share one axis by construction'.

**Mechanism.** two coordinate systems for one domain quantity, neither derived from the other

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p1.mjs: `BAR x=224.00 w=462.00`, handle 'Gradient stop at 0%' `centerX=235.00` (ramp paints 0% at x=224), handle 'Gradient stop at 100%' `centerX=675.00` (ramp paints 100% at x=686) → 11px = 2.38% of the ramp. evidence/p5.mjs (iPhone 14): bar w=324, centres 44/346 → 11px = 3.40%. evidence/p3.mjs T6: ten clicks at rail fractions .12 .20 .28 .36 .44 .52 .60 .72 .80 .88 produce `pos ["0","10","19","27","35","44","52","60","73","81","90","100"]` — click at 12.0% mints 10%, click at 88.0% mints 90%. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:317-326 (.gradient-rail background-size 100% 100%, origin/clip border-box), useGradientCSS.ts:266 (linear-gradient(90deg,…)), GradientStopEditor.vue:55-57 (handleLeft), :75-81 (getPosition), :8-13 and :49-53 (the false claim).
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p1.mjs (geometry) and evidence/p3.mjs block T6 (click→position map)

**Proposed cure.** One axis, expressed once. Either paint the ramp on the inset track (background-position: 11px 0; background-size: calc(100% - 22px) 100% — the pill caps then hold flat terminal colour, which is what a 0%/100% stop actually means), or put the handles on the full-width axis and inset the rail's silhouette instead. HANDLE_HALF must be read from the rendered handle box rather than re-declared as a JS literal (D-13).

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The save ownership seam drops the emitted palette name — GeneratePane.vue binds only the first emit payload and hard-codes the name literal, so the user-typed plate title is never persisted.

**Mechanism.** Broken ownership seam: the concept "name of the thing being saved" has two homes — a ref in the child (GenerateControls.vue:52) and a string literal in the parent — joined by a Vue emit whose payload TypeScript lets the parent silently ignore (narrower handler arity is legal).

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:48-50 `defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()` and :102-104 `emit("save", [...palette.value], paletteName.value)` vs demo/workbenches/generate/GeneratePane.vue:16-21 `function onSave(colors: string[]) { ... pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors); }`. Live probe output: {"typedName":"PROBE-NAME-XYZ","hits":[{"key":"color-palettes","hasProbeName":false,"hasHardcoded":true,"sample":"{\"version\":1,\"palettes\":[{\"id\":\"785315be-6955-405b-8e26-04d34cfcc55d\",\"name\":\"Generated Palette\",\"slug\":\"generated-palette-5fd9d37c\"..."}],"restored":true}
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate http://localhost:9000/#/generate; set the plate title input to any string and dispatch an input event; click button[aria-label="Save palette"]; read localStorage['color-palettes'] — the persisted palette's `name` is the literal "Generated Palette", the typed string is absent. Executed live with localStorage snapshot+restore.

**Proposed cure.** Immediate (CURABLE NOW, lands in GeneratePane.vue, outside the pin): bind the second emit parameter. Idiomatic: hoist to a provided `useGenerateSession()` under a typed InjectionKey at the pane — preset/harmony/count/seed/name/palette/regenerate/save/copyColors — so `save()` reads the name from the same object the input writes and the seam does not exist to fall through.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The stop-ordering invariant ("positions are non-decreasing") has three owners with three contradictory policies: gradientParse REJECTS violations, addStop SORTS, and updateStop — the mutator this component drives on every pointermove and arrow key — does NOTHING. The result is a live model whose own serializer emits CSS that its own parser rejects, and a rail ramp with decreasing sub-stop positions that CSS clamps into a flat band.

**Mechanism.** unique-semantic-ownership violated — a model invariant enforced by whichever mutator remembers it rather than by the type that carries it

**Evidence.**

```
Owners: gradientParse.ts:284-290 (reject) vs useGradientModel.ts:118 (sort) vs useGradientModel.ts:127-131 (no policy); driven by GradientStopEditor.vue:94,145,179 -> GradientVisualizer.vue:94-96. LIVE (localhost:9000/#/gradient, fresh mount, add stop at 50% then drag first handle to 80%): handles = ["Gradient stop at 80%","Gradient stop at 50%","Gradient stop at 100%"]; CSS readout = `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(0.75 0.15 145) 80%, oklch(70% 0.165 205deg) 50%, oklch(0.65 0.18 265) 100%)`; computed --rail-ramp = `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(75% …) 80.00%, oklch(74.68% …) 78.13%, oklch(74.37% …) 76.25%, …` (DECREASING). Screenshot: docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence-rail-after-reorder.png shows the rail as one flat green slab to ~78% with a hard band to blue. LIVE round trip: feeding that CSS back into [aria-label="Gradient CSS"] yields verdict "stop positions must be non-decreasing (hard-stop reordering isn't modeled)". HEADLESS: `npx vite-node` on the demo modules -> serialized `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(0.75 0.15 145) 70%, oklch(0.7 0.16 200) 50%, oklch(0.65 0.18 265) 100%)` -> re-parse REJECT with the same reason. Untested by the gates: e2e/smoke/views/gradient.spec.ts:161-172 drags only the MIDDLE handle to 75%, strictly between its neighbours.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/gradient -> click the rail at ~50% (adds a stop) -> drag the leftmost handle to ~80% -> read the CSS box: positions descend (80%, 50%, 100%). Retype that same string into the CSS box -> the destructive verdict appears. Headless equivalent: vite-node script calling updateStop-shaped mutation then serializeGradient -> parseGradientCSS.

**Proposed cure.** Brand the list. A model/stops.ts owns the ordering algebra and is the ONLY producer of an `OrderedStops` (readonly branded array): insertStop / moveStop (re-sorts, stable — the CSS-native behaviour a user expects from dragging past a neighbour) / removeStop. serializeGradient, sampleCoalescedStops and parseGradientCSS all take OrderedStops, so the parser's reject-branch becomes unreachable-by-construction instead of a runtime tripwire aimed at the app itself.

---

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The published `@mkbabb/value.js/css` surface is NOT total: parseCssColor throws a TypeError on eight empty-argument colour functions instead of returning {ok:false}. The demo's entire "library-oracle" architecture (gradientParse.ts header lines 16-17: "validated by the LIBRARY's own parsers — the validity oracles; the demo never hand-validates a color") is therefore structurally unsound, and the throw escapes the model-or-reject contract as an uncaught TypeError.

**Mechanism.** a published API whose type says ParseResult<CssColor> and whose runtime says "or I throw" — partiality hidden behind a non-null assertion; every consumer written against the type is structurally wrong

**Evidence.**

```
Root cause src/css/grammar.ts:181 `const components = splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g," "), "space");` — the non-null assertion over splitTopLevel("","/") which returns []. Consumer gradientParse.ts:92-94 `return parseCssColor(token).ok`. node on working-tree dist/subpaths/css.js: oklch()/rgb()/hsl()/lab()/lch()/oklab()/hwb()/color() all -> `THROW: TypeError Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')`; rgb(1 2 3)/red/oklch(0.7 0.1 30) -> ok. node on the INSTALLED node_modules/@mkbabb/value.js@4.0.0 -> same THROW (the bug is in the published package). In-browser dynamic import of the exact aliased module the demo runs -> {"oklch()":"THROW: …","rgb()":"THROW: …","oklch(0.7 0.1 30)":"ok"}. Through the demo parser (npx vite-node): `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(), blue)` -> UNCAUGHT TypeError; `linear-gradient(90deg, rgb(), blue)` -> UNCAUGHT; `linear-gradient(90deg, color(), blue)` -> UNCAUGHT; `linear-gradient(90deg, red, blue)` -> ok.
```

**Reproduction.** npx vite-node with `import { parseGradientCSS } from 'demo/workbenches/gradient/composables/gradientParse'; parseGradientCSS('linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(), blue)')` -> uncaught TypeError. Or type that string into the gradient CSS box on a mounted pane.

**Proposed cure.** Totality is the library's contract, not the consumer's try/catch. Replace the non-null assertions in parseFunctionalColor with an explicit empty-body reject (failure(source,"css_syntax",["components"])), and add a property test over {rgb,rgba,hsl,hsla,hwb,lab,lch,oklab,oklch,color} × {""," ","/",",,"} so the surface can never regress to partiality. Do NOT wrap isColorToken in try/catch — that moves the library's contract into its consumer.

---

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-0 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `<Button variant="…">` is inert against glass-ui 7 — the component's declared visual hierarchy never renders. glass-ui 7.0.0's ButtonProps has NO `variant` prop; its axis is `emphasis` (primary|secondary|quiet|text) plus `tone`. Undeclared props fall through to $attrs as inert DOM attributes, so GenerateControls' three Buttons all render at default emphasis. Repo-wide epidemic: 51 dead-prop Button sites vs 2 on the live API.

**Mechanism.** A design-system prop contract enforced by neither the type system nor the runtime. The producer may rename any prop and every consumer keeps compiling and keeps rendering something — worse than breaking, because it is invisible. Aggravated by glass-ui 7's own internal inconsistency: Slider and Badge kept `variant`, only Button moved to `emphasis`.

**Evidence.**

```
node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/button/Button.vue.d.ts:4 `export type ButtonEmphasis = "primary"|"secondary"|"quiet"|"text"` with no `variant` member in ButtonProps. `grep -o 'variant' dist/button-Bu9F4uU6.js | wc -l` → 0; `grep -o 'emphasis' …` → 6. `grep -o 'data-emphasis[^ {,]*' dist/components/button/styles.css | sort -u` → only the 4 emphasis values. `grep -c 'audacious' dist/components/button/styles.css` → 0. Call sites: GenerateControls.vue:158 variant="primary-audacious", :166 variant="ghost", :176 variant="ghost". Census: `grep -rn '<Button' -A4 demo/ | grep 'variant=' | wc -l` → 51 (16 ghost, 15 outline, 2 primary-audacious); `grep -rln 'emphasis=' demo/ | wc -l` → 2. `npx vue-tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit` → exit 0, no output (green with all 51 sites present).
```

**Reproduction.** Read docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png: "Regenerate" and the two icon buttons beside it render at IDENTICAL pale weight — three default-emphasis buttons, not one saturated primary flanked by two ghosts. Confirm the cause with the four greps above against glass-ui 7.0.0 in node_modules.

**Proposed cure.** Producer-first, and it belongs in the Glass 8 release gate (G-3), not a 51-site consumer sweep that rots on the next rename: glass-ui closes ButtonProps to unknown attributes, or asserts in dev when a `variant` attr reaches a Button root. Acceptance: `<Button variant="ghost">` FAILS at typecheck or first render, reproducibly in CI. Secondarily reconcile the axis naming across Button/Slider/Badge, since the inconsistency is what made 51 sites wrong by default. Only then sweep the consumers (variant="primary-audacious" → emphasis="primary", variant="ghost" → emphasis="quiet"), leaving Slider/Badge `variant` untouched.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The save seam silently discards the palette name the user types. GenerateControls emits `save: [colors, name]` and passes paletteName, but its sole consumer GeneratePane declares a one-parameter handler and hardcodes the literal "Generated Palette". The editable plate title has no effect on the saved palette.

**Mechanism.** The concept "the name of the thing being saved" has two homes — a ref inside the child and a string literal inside the parent — joined by a Vue emit whose payload the parent is free to ignore silently.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:48-50 `defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()`; :102-104 `emit("save", [...palette.value], paletteName.value)`. GeneratePane.vue:14-20 `function onSave(colors: string[])` — `name` never bound — then `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors)`. TypeScript permits a narrower handler arity, so nothing fails at build. The in-file comment at GenerateControls.vue:44-47 asserts the wire exists ("this emit is already truthful") — the emit is truthful; the wire was never landed.
```

**Reproduction.** Prior seat's live run against http://localhost:9000/#/generate: typed PROBE-NAME-XYZ into the plate title, clicked Save, read localStorage → {"hasProbeName": false, "hasHardcoded": true, sample name:"Generated Palette", slug:"generated-palette-5fd9d37c"}. Static confirmation needs no browser: GeneratePane.vue:14 never destructures the second emit parameter. My own independent re-probe was abandoned — the shared Playwright MCP browser was concurrently driven by parallel seats (my tab was navigated to #/mix mid-evaluation), and I discarded the run rather than report contaminated telemetry.

**Proposed cure.** CURABLE NOW (lands in GeneratePane.vue, outside the pin): bind the second parameter. The idiomatic cure is the L-6 transposition — the name belongs in a provided generate-session where there is no seam to drop it across, making the defect unrepresentable.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L2-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The per-swatch copy verb is dead: the component drives a WatercolorDot API glass-ui 7.0.0 does not declare (`tag="button"`), so five interactive swatches render as inert aria-hidden spans — not focusable, not clickable, invisible to AT. Copying a single generated colour is not a feature of the shipped product.

**Mechanism.** Producer/consumer prop contract enforced by neither the type system (Vue fallthrough attrs always type-check) nor the runtime (inheritAttrs:false silently eats them). Identical mechanism to pass-1 §L-0 (`<Button variant>`, 51 sites) — but a structural prop rather than a style prop, so the element is the wrong element and the behaviour never attaches.

**Evidence.**

```
CONSUMER: demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:199-208 (`tag="button"`, `:aria-label="`Copy ${css}`"`, `@click="copyColor(css)"`). PRODUCER: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/watercolor-dot/WatercolorDot.vue.d.ts — props = {color, variant, animate, cycleDuration, range, seed}; no `tag`, no emits. `grep -o inheritAttrs .../dist/watercolor-dot.js` → present; `grep -o 'aria-hidden[^,]{0,20}'` → `aria-hidden": "true"` ×3. MEASURED DOM: {"tag":"SPAN","attrs":["data-v-292b9032=","aria-hidden=true","class=generate-swatch w-9 h-9 sm:w-10 sm:h-10 ","data-testid=watercolor-swatch","data-variant=solid","style=background-color: oklch(…"]}. MEASURED FOCUS: {"matchesFV":false,"isActiveEl":false}. MEASURED CLICK: {"afterSwatchClick":[],"afterCopyAllClick":["oklch(76.364581747912% …"]}. Plate focusable census: ["INPUT:Palette name","BUTTON:Regenerate","BUTTON:Save palette","BUTTON:Copy all colors"] — zero swatches. GATES: `time npx vue-tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit` → EXIT=0 in 9.2s; e2e/smoke/oracles/o20-generate-plate.spec.ts:97-105 asserts only getComputedStyle().backgroundColor; docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/capture.mjs:88-98 selects only a,button,input,…,[tabindex] so an aria-hidden span is unobservable (REPORT.json generate rows: namelessButtons 0).
```

**Reproduction.** Playwright/WebKit against http://localhost:9000/#/generate (self-healing hash idiom, §8.6 of the report): (1) `swatch.focus()` → document.activeElement !== swatch; (2) patch navigator.clipboard.writeText to record calls, `document.querySelector('.generate-swatch').click()` → [] writes; (3) control: `button[aria-label="Copy all colors"].click()` → 1 write.

**Proposed cure.** The dot is decorative by the producer's own design (aria-hidden hardcoded) and should stay so. Consumer owns the verb seat: `<button aria-label="Copy …" @click><WatercolorDot :color :seed/></button>` — which also honours T-28's outline law honestly (the button carries the ring at the silhouette; the dot never does). BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8: new gate G-4 requires glass-ui to DECIDE (a) decorative-only + documented, or (b) typed polymorphic root + declared click emit, before any consumer edit. Widen gate G-3 from 'Button variant fails' to 'any attribute reaching any glass-ui component root outside its declared prop surface fails at typecheck or first render, reproducibly in CI'. Add O-20 behavioural assertion: swatch click → clipboard receives that swatch's CSS string.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 (pass-1, CONFIRMED LIVE by pass 2) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The palette name the user types is silently discarded on save: the child emits `save: [colors, name]` but the pane's handler declares arity 1 and passes the literal "Generated Palette" to the persistence port. Data loss on user input.

**Mechanism.** Ownership split with an untyped joint: the NAME is owned by the leaf component, the PERSISTENCE PORT (LIBRARY_PORT_KEY) is injected by the parent, and the two are joined by a Vue emit. TypeScript cannot catch it — a handler is always assignable to an emit with more parameters. Second symptom of the same split: `string[] → PaletteColor[]` is synthesised twice (GenerateControls.vue:55-57 and GeneratePane.vue:15-18) because neither file owns 'the generated palette as a domain object'.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:48-50 `defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()`, :52 `paletteName = ref("Generated Palette")`, :102-104 `emit("save", [...palette.value], paletteName.value)`. GeneratePane.vue:14 `function onSave(colors: string[])` — name parameter never bound — and :19 `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors)`. LIVE REPRODUCTION (pass 2, which pass 1 had to abandon under shared-browser contention): {"url":"http://localhost:9000/#/generate","inputAfterSet":"ZZ-PROBE-NAME","nameMatches":[["color-palettes",["Generated Palette","Sunset","Empty Plate","Overflowing"]]]} — the v-model round-trip is verified and the persisted store contains zero occurrences of ZZ-PROBE-NAME. Comment at :44-47 asserts 'this emit is already truthful' — it is not.
```

**Reproduction.** Playwright at /#/generate with the self-healing hash idiom: set input[aria-label="Palette name"] to ZZ-PROBE-NAME via the native value setter + input event (verified round-trip), click button[aria-label="Save palette"], read localStorage['color-palettes'] → names are ["Generated Palette","Sunset","Empty Plate","Overflowing"].

**Proposed cure.** ESCALATION RECOMMENDED — this is the highest-value action available before Glass 8. GeneratePane.vue is NOT in the hold's pinned receiver set (the four pinned files are ComponentSliders.vue, ConfigSliderPane.vue, ExtractControls.vue, GenerateControls.vue), so binding the second emit argument at GeneratePane.vue:14-19 cures the data loss WITHOUT touching any pinned file. The full cure is the session hoist: `useGenerateSession()` owning preset/harmony/count/seed/name/palette/regenerate/save, provided at the pane under a typed InjectionKey — one writer, one adapter, and the name-drop becomes unrepresentable.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-1 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The published library declares its own demo's design system as a runtime dependency, forming a declared package cycle and forcing 5.8 MB of unused install weight on every consumer.

**Mechanism.** wrong-direction dependency declared at the package manifest level: demo-only tooling placed in `dependencies` instead of `devDependencies`, creating value.js → glass-ui → value.js.

**Evidence.**

```
package.json:82-85 `"dependencies": {"@mkbabb/glass-ui":"^7.0.0","@mkbabb/keyframes.js":"^6.0.0"}` — the ONLY two entries. Measured: `grep -rn "@mkbabb/glass-ui|@mkbabb/keyframes" src/ | wc -l` → 0; `grep -rn "@mkbabb/glass-ui" dist/subpaths/*.js dist/value.js | wc -l` → 0; `grep -rn "@mkbabb/keyframes" demo/ | wc -l` → 0 (referenced by NOTHING in the repo). `du -sh node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui node_modules/@mkbabb/keyframes.js` → 5.2M + 608K, against dist/subpaths at 132K. glass-ui/package.json peerDependencies includes `"@mkbabb/value.js":"^4.0.0"`.
```

**Reproduction.** `npm i @mkbabb/value.js@4.0.0` in an empty non-Vue project: the install pulls a Vue design system plus a keyframes engine and glass-ui's own peer set (vue, reka-ui, embla-carousel, tailwindcss, @mkbabb/pencil-boil). `import { CubicBezier } from "@mkbabb/value.js/easing"` uses none of them.

**Proposed cure.** Move both entries to `devDependencies`. `dependencies` becomes `{}` — the honest shape for a zero-runtime-dependency value library. Nothing breaks: `"files": ["dist", "!dist/gh-pages", …]` (package.json:49-53) never ships the demo.
