### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-8 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The view-change channel carries two disjoint meanings — a ViewId and the magic sentinel `"__admin_toggle__"` — and the far end casts unvalidated into `router.push` while the type predicate written for exactly that check is never called on the write path.

**Mechanism.** one channel, two message types (navigate | toggle-mode) plus an unchecked cast at the module boundary

**Evidence.**

```
DockViewSelect.vue:105 `<SelectItem value="__admin_toggle__" ...>`. useDockAdminMode.ts:65-73: `function onViewChange(id: string | number | boolean | Record<string, string> | null) { if (typeof id === "string") { if (id === "__admin_toggle__") { toggleAdminMode(); return; } viewManager.switchView(id as ViewId); } }` — the cast lands in `router.push({ name: id })` at useViewManager.ts:79. viewSchema.ts:236 exports `isViewId`, and `grep -rn "isViewId" demo/` shows it used only on the READ path (useViewManager.ts:45, useDocumentTitle.ts:50). Doubled defensive typing that validates nothing: DockViewSelect.vue:80 already narrows with `emit('update:modelValue', id as string)` and declares the emit `[id: string]` at :26, yet the receiver re-widens to reka's full AcceptableValue union.
```

**Reproduction.** sed -n '65,73p' demo/shell/dock/composables/useDockAdminMode.ts; grep -rn "isViewId" demo/ shows no call site on the write path.

**Proposed cure.** Split the channel: `emits: { navigate: [ViewId]; toggleAdmin: [] }`. Type `switchView(id: ViewId)` and let callers narrow with `isViewId` — no cast, no sentinel, no magic string with no home.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-18 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The chrome-tier ink referent probes a node that paints nothing: `resolveLiveTint("chrome")` reads `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('.glass-dock')).backgroundColor`, which is fully transparent under Glass 7 — the glass is painted on the `.dock-plate` child. The live probe returns undefined on every call and the static model silently serves.

**Mechanism.** Cross-package boundary violation: a demo feature module reaches a producer's internal paint value through a DOM class-name query instead of a published token/contract. Untyped, unexported, untested — so a producer paint change (Glass 7, adopted whole at V·W44) un-wires it silently.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/color-session/useContrastSafeColor.ts:150-153 (the querySelector) and :217 (`if (!resolved || resolved.alpha === 0) return undefined`). Measured live (scratchpad L-probe5.mjs, /#/, 1280x900, dock expanded): light {"dockBg":"rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)","plateBg":"color(srgb 0.931227 0.845921 0.816039 / 0.5392)"} ; dark {"dockBg":"rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)","plateBg":"color(srgb 0.390298 0.334077 0.29024 / 0.5776)"}. Producer paint site: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/dock/styles/morph.css `.glass-dock:not(.vertical) .dock-plate { background: color-mix(...) }`. Static fallback now in force: demo/color-session/ink.ts:29-35 RUNG_ALPHA.floating = {light 0.8, dark 0.88}, FLOATING_TINT_L = {light 1, dark 0.345}, applied to chrome at ink.ts:118-122. Sole consumer of the tier: `grep -rn 'useSafeAccentFn("chrome")' demo` -> demo/shell/dock/menus/ProfileSection.vue:28.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe5.mjs against the live dev server on localhost:9000 — prints dockBg rgba(0,0,0,0) and the real plateBg with alpha 0.5392/0.5776 in light/dark. Consequence example (labelled: assumed ambient L 0.75): surfaceLightness = a*L + (1-a)*ambient gives 0.95 modelled vs 0.83 measured — ink certified against a surface an eighth of the OKLab range too light. e2e/smoke/oracles/o18-contrast-census.spec.ts:594-635 measures only the outcome at one boot colour and never asserts the live probe returned anything.

**Proposed cure.** The referent is the producer's own composited value, so the producer publishes it: glass-ui exposes `--glass-tint-chrome` (resolved) or `useDockSurfaceTint()` on the existing DockContext, and the demo reads a token, never a node. The `chrome` branch — and the identical `querySelector('[data-surface="veil"]')` idiom at :170-176 — then collapse to token reads with no DOM archaeology. Interim `.glass-dock .dock-plate` is another line of the same wrong kind and will break on the next paint change. Secondary: the selector is document-order dependent — two `.glass-dock` elements exist on /#/atmosphere and /#/blob (demo/scenes/ConfigSliderPane.vue:164); measured firstIsShell:true today, breakage on re-order is a hypothesis.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-19 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `isAdminMode` is a one-way latch defined by two disagreeing predicates. After one visit to a tuning route the dock wears admin identity (gold shimmer glyph + gold `--dock-ring`) on every subsequent non-admin route for the rest of the session.

**Mechanism.** Duplicated state for a value that is a pure function of the route, with the predicate implemented twice and neither copy owned by the schema that owns views (demo/shell/viewSchema.ts).

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/composables/useDockAdminMode.ts:32 seeds the ref with a PREFIX test `currentView.startsWith("admin-")`; :55-59 maintains it with a MEMBERSHIP test `adminViews.includes(view)` and has no falsifying arm. The two predicates disagree on `atmosphere`/`blob` (in the array at :27, not prefixed). Reproduced live (scratchpad L-probe6.mjs, headless Chromium 1280x900): `1 fresh picker {"route":"#/","goldIcon":false,"ring":"oklch(0.471189 0.188448 9.8340"}` / `2 -> atmosphere {"goldIcon":true,"ring":"light-dark(oklch(0.751 0.147 8"}` / `3 back() -> picker {"route":"#/","goldIcon":true,"ring":"light-dark(oklch(0.751 0.147 8"}` / `4 -> palettes {"goldIcon":true,...}`.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe6.mjs — load /#/ (gold false), hash to /#/atmosphere (gold true), page.goBack() to /#/ (gold STILL true), hash to /#/palettes (gold STILL true). With admin credentials the damage is functional, not cosmetic: viewEntries (:34-39) then returns only the seven admin views while on /#/palettes, so the dock's own navigation cannot reach the user views except through the `__admin_toggle__` sentinel row.

**Proposed cure.** Derive, do not latch. Add `group: "user" | "admin"` to `PaneConfig` (viewSchema.ts owns the partition) and reduce the composable to `const isAdminMode = computed(() => VIEW_MAP[viewManager.currentView.value].group === "admin")`. The ref, both watchers and both hardcoded ViewId arrays die; `toggleAdminMode` keeps its real job (it navigates) and the flag follows the route by construction — the same transposition `useViewManager.ts:51-58` already applied to `mobilePaneIndex`.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-20 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The local `useLayerTransition` shim in ActionBarLayer produces no crossfade at all: the producer CSS the template hand-binds declares no opacity transition, so the 260 ms timer window is an instant cut with a transparent inert box held over the live controls.

**Mechanism.** A local fork of a retired producer concept, styled out of the producer's private stylesheet by class-name convention; the fork reimplements the packaging but cannot reach the animation, which the producer moved behind a component.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/layers/ActionBarLayer.vue:62-94 (the shim, SUB_LAYER_CROSSFADE_MS = 260, subLayerProps hand-binding `.dock-layer`/`is-active`/`is-leaving`). Producer rules: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/dock/styles/layers.css declares only `transition: visibility 0s linear var(--duration-normal)` on `.dock-layer`; the opacity overlap lives on `.dock-crossfade[data-crossfading] > .dock-face` via `--dock-t` (crossfade.css), reachable only inside `<DockCrossfade>`. Measured live (scratchpad L-probe2.mjs), both faces at rest / +60ms / +460ms across the actions->input swap: `transitionProperty: "visibility", transitionDuration: "0s"` at every phase; opacity steps 1->0 and 0->1 in one frame; the leaving face holds a 442x45 transparent inert box. The public successor exists: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/components/dock/index.d.ts:5 `export { default as DockCrossfade } from "./DockCrossfade.vue";` — contradicting the shim's own comment at ActionBarLayer.vue:54-61.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe2.mjs — navigate /#/, click [aria-label='Toggle action bar'], click [aria-label='Open color input'], sample getComputedStyle on both children of `.dock-layer-grid` at 60 ms and 460 ms. transition-property is `visibility` and duration `0s` in all four phases.

**Proposed cure.** Replace ActionBarLayer.vue:62-101 with `<DockCrossfade :active="activeSubLayer" reserve="inline">` wrapping two `<DockLayer id="actions">` / `<DockLayer id="input">` faces. The shim, the 260 ms literal, the timer, `subLayerProps`, `subLayerGridEl`, the `void opts.containerEl` parity argument and the hand-bound producer classes all die (~40 lines), and the producer's spring-driven overlap, peak-size reserve and focus transfer from a dissolving face arrive instead of being approximated.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-6 (carried, independently re-verified) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Inverted dependency: the shell's persistent chrome imports its identity/session port from the palettes FEATURE, putting 23 palettes modules into the dock's runtime graph on every route.

**Mechanism.** A feature owns a platform concept; the shell must reach down into the feature to render chrome, creating the palettes<->shell module cycle.

**Evidence.**

```
Five of the six `SESSION_PORT_KEY` consumers are dock files: demo/shell/dock/Dock.vue:18, DockViewSelect.vue:8, layers/SlugEditLayer.vue:5, menus/ProfileSection.vue:14, menus/MobileMenuDropdown.vue:13 — the sixth is the definition in demo/palettes/usePalettePorts.ts. Runtime-only import-graph walk from Dock.vue (scratchpad L-graph2.mjs, `import type` edges correctly excluded as erased): 65 modules total, area histogram {"demo/shell":20,"demo/color-session":6,"demo/ui":7,"demo/platform":9,"demo/palettes":23}. The payload is pure identity (usePalettePorts.ts Port 1: isAdminAuthenticated, userSlug, userLogout, ensureUser, ensureSession, onRegenerateSlug, onSlugSwitch) assembled from demo/platform/auth/{useAdminAuth,useUserAuth,useSession} — which already exist as the proper home.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-graph2.mjs — 23 of the dock's 65 runtime modules are demo/palettes. The picker, admin and 404 routes all instantiate palette machinery to render a Login button.

**Proposed cure.** Move the port to demo/platform/auth/session.ts exporting `SESSION_KEY: InjectionKey<Session>`, provided at the composition root; usePalettePorts consumes it like every other consumer. `shell -> palettes` goes 5 -> 0 and the cycle dissolves.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure / module boundaries / owners` · L-7 (carried, sharpened with a live measurement) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** MT-F005 located: the app's single nameless button is a hand-rolled `<button>` inside the dock's ColorInput — the one control in the dock subtree that bypasses the design-system primitive, and therefore the one that lost the accessible name the primitive enforces.

**Mechanism.** Bypassing the design-system primitive loses the affordance the primitive enforces; the dock's own written law (Dock.vue:140-142, 'icon-only controls carry aria-label') is obeyed everywhere it goes through DockControl and violated in the one place it does not.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/shell/dock/ColorInput.vue:67-82 — both branches are `<button class="send-btn btn-interactive">` with only an SVG child, no aria-label, no text, no title. It is the only nameless control: all 8 `<DockControl>` uses in the subtree carry aria-label (Dock.vue x3, ActionBarToggle x1, SlugEditLayer x3, ActionBarLayer x1) and the other hand-rolled button, ActionButton.vue:13, carries `:aria-label="title"`. Measured live after two clicks (scratchpad L-probe3.mjs: Toggle action bar -> Open color input): `send-btn: {"visible":true,"ariaLabel":null,"text":"","title":null,"rect":[24,24],"inertAncestor":false,"cs":{"visibility":"visible","opacity":"1"}}`. Nameless-button enumeration at rest, 1280x900 /#/: 1 hit, path `div.dock-face-content > div.flex.items-center.gap-0 > div.dock-layer-grid.flex-1 > ... > button.send-btn.btn-interactive`.
```

**Reproduction.** node scratchpad/L-probe3.mjs — load /#/, click [aria-label='Toggle action bar'], click [aria-label='Open color input'], then read button.send-btn: visible, 24x24, accessible name null, no inert ancestor. Two clicks from cold boot.

**Proposed cure.** Route the send affordance through `DockControl` (or glass-ui `Button`), which carries the aria-label contract; the 24x24 box also sits at the WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8 floor, and since `compact` is a producer variant the size cure belongs producer-side (edict 5), not as a per-instance override.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D3 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The plate's editable title is discarded at the emit seam. GenerateControls emits `save: [colors, name]` but GeneratePane's handler declares only `colors` and hardcodes `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", …)`. Renaming the plate does nothing; the UI shows one name and saves another.

**Mechanism.** TypeScript cannot catch a handler with fewer parameters — it is assignable — so the emit contract is unenforced at the only seam that matters.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:44-50,102-104 (comment: "this emit is already truthful") vs demo/workbenches/generate/GeneratePane.vue:14,19. Live: typed "MY CUSTOM NAME", clicked Save → bodyText "My Palettes 2 (2 saved) … Generated Palette 5   Generated Palette 5".
```

**Reproduction.** At http://localhost:9000/#/generate set the plate input to 'MY CUSTOM NAME' via a native input event, click the 'Save palette' button, read the Palettes pane: the saved entry reads 'Generated Palette'.

**Proposed cure.** Consume the emitted name (`function onSave(colors: string[], name: string)`), and guard the empty string at the emit site rather than the sink — a decorative rename field is worse than no field. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

---

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D4 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** `variant` is not a glass-ui 7.0.0 Button prop. `variant="primary-audacious"` and two `variant="ghost"` fall through $attrs as junk DOM attributes; all three plate verbs render at the default `emphasis="secondary" tone="neutral"`, so the documented "deliberate-primary register" and the two ghost icon buttons are visually identical.

**Mechanism.** Vue fallthrough attrs make an unknown component prop a silent no-op that vue-tsc cannot flag; the W44 glass-7 adoption migrated the pin but not the emphasis axis — demo-wide (27 files use `variant=`, only 2 occurrences of `emphasis=` exist).

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:158,167,176 vs dist/components/button/Button.vue.d.ts (`ButtonProps { emphasis?: "primary"|"secondary"|"quiet"|"text"; tone?; size?; iconOnly?; … }` — no `variant`). `grep -rl "primary-audacious" node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist --include="*.js"` → nothing. Live DOM: all three buttons carry `data-emphasis=secondary` plus a raw `variant=…` attribute and the `glass-wash glass-capsule glass-capsule-hover` classes glass-ui applies only to neutral primary/secondary. Visual: shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png — Regenerate reads identically to the icon buttons, unlike the dock's real primary Login pill.
```

**Reproduction.** Load /#/generate; evaluate [...document.querySelectorAll('[data-generate-plate] button')].map(b=>[...b.attributes].map(a=>a.name+'='+a.value).join(' | ')) — every row shows data-emphasis=secondary alongside the dead variant attribute.

**Proposed cure.** `emphasis="primary"` on Regenerate, `emphasis="quiet"` on the two icon buttons; delete `variant` outright — no alias, no compat shim (edict 2). Structurally, only an eslint rule (or making the barrels typed wrappers) can turn unknown component attributes into errors; relay the API break to the glass-ui BH inbox. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D5 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Both clipboard verbs discard the discriminated CopyResult that glass-ui deliberately made non-boolean, and the component emits no success or failure feedback for any async verb — no aria-live, no toast, nothing.

**Mechanism.** A Result whose failure arm is dropped is indistinguishable from success; on an insecure origin (LAN dev with server.host:true, and the mbabb.fi.ncsu.edu/colors/ deployment) `no-api` is swallowed and the user believes the copy landed.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:106-113 (`await writeClipboard(...)`, result unbound) vs dist/composables/dom/useClipboard.d.ts: `type CopyResult = {ok:true}|{ok:false;reason:"clipboard-api"|"no-api"}` with the doc "Returns the discriminated result … rather than a lossy boolean, for identical call ergonomics: const { ok } = await writeClipboard(text)". `grep -rn "aria-live|role=\"status\"|toast" demo/workbenches/generate/` → no matches. Live save probe produced no announcement and no in-plate change.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE for the failure arm (I did not force an insecure origin) — the dropped-Result and absent-live-region facts are CONFIRMED by the pasted contract and the empty grep; the insecure-origin consequence is labelled a hypothesis.

**Proposed cure.** Route Save and both Copy verbs through glass-ui's `useClipboard()` and bind its `status` ref to ONE `aria-live="polite"` region inside the plate — one region, three verbs, no per-instance chrome, no new shared/ dir. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D6 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Two unchecked `as PresetName` / `as HarmonyName` casts of reka-ui's `AcceptableValue` (which admits null), with asymmetric failure modes: an invalid preset THROWS inside the `palette` computed and blanks the pane; an invalid harmony silently yields an EMPTY palette that save() will happily persist.

**Mechanism.** A cast is not a check. The throw escalates from a computed read into the render function — the shipped-blank class already in this repo's record; the silent-[] arm is worse still because save() at :103 emits [] with no guard at either end.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:75-81; generate-color.ts:220,227 (`GENERATION_PRESETS[preset]` unguarded, then `p.l[0]`) and :101-146 (`switch` with NO default → returns []). Executed against an esbuild bundle of the real module: `invalid preset -> THREW TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'l')`; `invalid harmony -> array len 0`; `count 0/-1/NaN -> array len 0`; `count 1.5 -> array len 2`.
```

**Reproduction.** node on a bundled demo/color-session/generate-color.ts: generatePalette(5,"nope","golden",1) throws TypeError; generatePalette(5,"vibrant","bogus",1) returns []. Reachability of a non-PresetName value THROUGH reka-ui is a HYPOTHESIS — I did not force reka-ui to emit null; the crash mechanism itself is CONFIRMED.

**Proposed cure.** Validate at the boundary against the already-exported PRESET_NAMES / HARMONY_NAMES and ignore anything else — one narrow function per select, zero casts. In the core, make `generateHues` exhaustive (a `never` arm) and the preset lookup non-optional, so the two paths cannot diverge into crash-vs-silence again. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · D7 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The plate chrome row wraps at 1440 px desktop — overflowing by exactly 3 px — while the component's own comment claims the wrap only happens "at 390". The count badge is flung to the far right of line 1 and the plate grows ~40 px taller than designed at every width below ~1500 px.

**Mechanism.** `flex-wrap: wrap` breaks lines on hypothetical main sizes (flex-basis) BEFORE `flex-1` shrinking applies, so a 160 px basis forces a break the shrink would have absorbed.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:138-142 (the claim) and :148 (`flex-1 basis-[10rem]`). Measured live at the audit's own matrix (capture.mjs:45 → 1440×900): {"plateW":462,"rowContentW":436,"input":{"y":363,"w":398},"badge":{"x":643,"w":30},"cluster":{"y":399,"w":233},"wrapped":true}. Hypothetical line sum 160+30+233+16 = 439 > 436. Visible in shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png and safari-mobile-dark/generate.png.
```

**Reproduction.** Load http://localhost:9000/#/generate at viewport 1440×900; measure the `.flex-wrap` row's input and `.ml-auto` cluster bounding boxes — cluster.y (399) > input.y (363): wrapped.

**Proposed cure.** `basis-0` (or drop `basis-*` and keep `flex-1 min-w-0`) so the title lane's hypothetical size is zero and the row breaks only when the verb cluster itself cannot fit — one class, and the comment then becomes true. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · C3 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Every pointermove during a handle drag emits `update:position` TWICE — the bar's handler documents itself as a 'fallback path while a handle drag is live (capture sits on the handle)', but captured events still bubble to the bar, so both handlers always run.

**Mechanism.** A false premise about pointer capture (it retargets, it does not stop propagation) left a live duplicate code path that the comment records as fact.

**Evidence.**

```
Live counters on a 20-step drag: {"barMovesOnHandle":21,"barMovesTotal":22} — the bar's draggingId branch fired on every move of the drag. Code: GradientStopEditor.vue:91-99 (bar) and :136-146 (handle); each calls getPosition → getBoundingClientRect (line 77), a forced layout. Downstream cost measured via evidence/serialize-cost.ts: `per invalidation (all three computeds): 0.223 ms` at 3 stops (coalesced 0.110 / rail 0.111 / simple 0.001 ms).
```

**Reproduction.** Attach a bubble-phase pointermove listener to [data-testid=gradient-stop-bar], drag a handle 20 steps, count events whose target matches [data-stop-id].

**Proposed cure.** Delete the branch: `if (draggingId.value) return;` at the top of onBarPointerMove — with capture on the handle the bar has no job during a drag — and cache the rail rect at pointerdown instead of measuring twice per move (the rail cannot resize mid-drag; touch-action:none is already set).

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · C4 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Test truth: 5 of 13 gradient e2e tests FAIL at HEAD — including the ONLY test that exercises add/drag/remove — two of them asserting DOM retired ten days ago; no CI workflow runs Playwright at all; and the drag leg was vacuous even when green.

**Mechanism.** The gate was never wired to CI, so its specs decayed against a moving DOM; the surviving assertions are lower-bound checks that no plausible mutation can fail.

**Evidence.**

```
`npx playwright test e2e/smoke/views/gradient.spec.ts e2e/smoke/oracles/o21-gradient-rail.spec.ts --project=smoke --reporter=line` → `5 failed … 8 passed (4.2m)`; failures: o21:129 ruler grammar, views:40, views:106 (envelope plate), views:144 (stop add/drag/remove — `locator.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded: Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded … element is not stable`), views:270 (`steps(4, end)` expected, `steps(4, jump-end)` rendered). `grep -rn "gradient-ruler-cap\|gradient-rung" --include=*.vue --include=*.ts demo/ src/` → no matches; `grep -rn "Perceived-space" demo/` → no matches; both retired in a68ecdc1 (2026-07-17). `grep -rn playwright .github/workflows/*.yml` → nothing. No unit test references the component.
```

**Reproduction.** Run the two spec files as above; grep the retired testids; grep the workflows.

**Proposed cure.** Wire the smoke project into CI as a hard gate, re-anchor the three stale specs to living DOM, and replace the drag leg's `expect(pct).toBeGreaterThan(60)` with the invariants that actually matter: after any drag the model is sorted, the rail's ramp positions are non-decreasing, and `simpleCSS` round-trips through parseGradientCSS. Exact mutation that keeps today's suite green: set `HANDLE_HALF = 0` (GradientStopEditor.vue:53), deleting the entire inset-track geometry the file is architected around.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · C5 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The gradient route's parse path THROWS instead of rejecting on the 8 empty-argument colour functions (MT-F001): the throw escapes parseGradientCSS → applyCSS → onParseCSS into a debounce timer, so the 'model-or-reject, fail LOUD' contract inverts into an uncaught TypeError with no verdict rendered.

**Mechanism.** A `!` assertion standing in for a missing empty-input branch turns a Result-returning oracle into a partial function, and every caller that trusted the Result contract inherits an uncatchable throw.

**Evidence.**

```
`npx vite-node .../evidence/parse-probe.ts` → `parseCssColor("oklch()") -> THROW TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')` and identically for rgb()/hsl()/lab()/lch()/oklab()/hwb()/color(); `parseGradientCSS -> THROW TypeError … << linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(), blue)`. Root: src/css/grammar.ts:181 `splitTopLevel(slash[0]!.replace(/,/g, " "), "space")` where splitTopLevel (grammar.ts:63-87) returns [] for an empty body, so the non-null assertion is false. Consumer: demo/workbenches/gradient/composables/gradientParse.ts:92 treats parseCssColor as a total oracle; GradientVisualizer.vue:102-108 has no try; GradientCodeEditor.vue:55-57 runs it inside a 500ms debounce timer.
```

**Reproduction.** Run the parse probe; or type `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(), blue)` into the Gradient CSS editor and watch for an uncaught TypeError with no verdict paragraph.

**Proposed cure.** Fix it in grammar.ts, not the pane: `const head = slash[0]; if (head === undefined) return failure(source, "css_syntax", ["CSS color"]);`. A try/catch in onParseCSS would be a masking fallback (edict 2) and would leave every other parseCssColor caller exposed.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defect hunt on demo/workbenches` · C6 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Accessibility is decorative, not operational: there is NO keyboard path to add a stop; the handles implement the slider keyboard pattern on a bare <button> with no role/aria-value*; focus is destroyed on remove; and the visible targets measure 20x20.

**Mechanism.** The keyboard affordances were bolted onto the button role rather than the component adopting the semantics of what it is (a multi-thumb slider over a rail), so AT is told 'button' while the code behaves like a slider.

**Evidence.**

```
Live focusable census inside main on /#/gradient: ["Gradient stop at 0%","Gradient stop at 100%","Gradient type","Interpolation space","Hue interpolation","Gradient direction", …] — the rail div measures role:null, tabindex:null, so click-to-add is pointer-exclusive (WCAG 2.1.1). Handles measure role:null with no aria-valuenow/min/max while GradientStopEditor.vue:173-187 implements arrow nudges (WCAG 4.1.2); no aria-live for add/remove. removeStop (:158-162) unmounts the focused handle / the chip (`v-if="selectedStop && removable"`) with no focus restoration → focus falls to body (WCAG 2.4.3). REPORT.json /#/gradient (all 4 matrices): {"w":20,"h":20,"label":"Gradient stop at 0%"} and {"w":20,"h":20,"label":"Gradient stop at 100%"} = 2 of the route's 6 small tap targets, 8 of the audit's 60 rows.
```

**Reproduction.** Tab through /#/gradient and try to create a stop; inspect a handle's role/aria attributes; press Delete on a focused handle and query document.activeElement.

**Proposed cure.** Give each handle role="slider" with aria-valuemin/max/now/text bounded by its neighbours, add Home/End/PageUp/PageDown, make the rail itself focusable with Enter/Space (or an explicit 'Add stop' control) to mint at the focused position, announce results through one aria-live region, and move focus to the neighbouring handle after a remove. Raise the visible dot to 24px so the hit region and the target agree.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/workbenches/gen` · C-5 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** Gate vacuity, three ways: the component's ONLY oracle (O-20) is currently RED for an unrelated serialization-form reason, CI never runs e2e at all, there are zero unit tests for the generation core, and `npm run typecheck` is green over every defect here.

**Mechanism.** Oracles assert decorative properties (a forwarded class + computed backgroundColor) instead of the control contract (role, accessible name, operability), so they are indifferent to whether the control exists; and the one oracle that would fail is neither run by CI nor currently green.

**Evidence.**

```
`npx playwright test e2e/smoke/oracles/o20-generate-plate.spec.ts --reporter=line` → "1 failed, 1 passed (39.2s)", failing at o20:105 with stamped "oklch(85.53637546394% 0.087179123785 49.783647190779deg)" vs live "oklch(0.855364 0.0871791 49.7836)" (numerically IDENTICAL — serializeCssColor vs getComputedStyle forms) · `grep -n "run:" .github/workflows/ci.yml` → npm ci / lint / vue-tsc lib / vue-tsc demo / build / npm test / verify-packed-surface / tsc / npm test — no playwright step in any of ci.yml, deploy-pages.yml, release.yml · `grep -rln "GenerateControls|useColorGeneration|generatePalette|generateSingleColor" test e2e` → only o20-generate-plate.spec.ts · `npx vue-tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit; echo EXIT=$?` → EXIT=0 with --listFiles confirming both GenerateControls.vue and WatercolorDot.vue.d.ts in the program · o27-focus-affordance.spec.ts:33-41 pins its WCAG-2.5.8 BR-3 rule to openGradient() only
```

**Reproduction.** MUTATION TEST — delete `tag="button"`, `:aria-label` and `@click` from GenerateControls.vue:199-208 and re-run O-20: the result is unchanged (test 1 still passes, test 2 still fails for its own serialization reason). o20:98-105 selects `.generate-swatch` (a class WatercolorDot DOES forward) and reads getComputedStyle(el).backgroundColor (painted by the producer regardless).

**Proposed cure.** Assert the contract, not the paint: O-20's swatch leg becomes role + accessible-name assertions; its byte-identity leg normalises both sides through one serializer. Generalise o27-focus-affordance.spec.ts from openGradient() to the route matrix so BR-3 (≥24px) and the focus-affordance rules cover every route. Put test:e2e in ci.yml or delete the suite — an unrun oracle manufactures the false confidence that let a 7.0.0 API break reach the shipped plate. All three land in e2e/ and touch no pinned consumer.

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/workbenches/gen` · C-2 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The plate's editable name is discarded on save — renaming the palette has no effect. GenerateControls emits `save: [colors, name]`; the only consumer declares a one-parameter handler and hardcodes the literal "Generated Palette".

**Mechanism.** Positional emit payload silently dropped by handler arity — TypeScript accepts a narrower handler as assignable to a wider emit signature, so vue-tsc cannot see the drop. The same drop applies to the dock-driven save (GeneratePane.vue:22-26 re-exposes save through the same emit).

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/GenerateControls.vue:48-50 (`defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()`) and :102-104 (`emit("save", [...palette.value], paletteName.value)`) · demo/workbenches/generate/GeneratePane.vue:14 `function onSave(colors: string[])` and :19 `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors)` · demo/palettes/usePaletteStore.ts:66 `function createPalette(name: string, colors: PaletteColor[])` — the name parameter exists and is ignored · LIVE: {"inputValueAfter":"MY UNIQUE NAME 4711", "namesBefore":["Generated Palette","Sunset",…], "namesAfter":["Generated Palette","Generated Palette","Sunset",…]}
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/generate → type any name into the plate title input → click the Save-palette button → read localStorage["color-palettes"]: the new entry is named "Generated Palette", not the typed name. Verified live with a native value setter + input event so v-model commits.

**Proposed cure.** Immediate: bind the payload — `onSave(colors: string[], name: string)` → `pm.createPalette(name, paletteColors)`. Structural cure that kills the class: emit a single object payload (`save: [{ colors, name }]`) so ignoring a field must be explicit rather than a consequence of arity. (GeneratePane.vue is not the pinned file, but no source edits land from this formation — authored as a wave item.)

### `CHALLENGE-C — implementation defects in demo/workbenches/gen` · C-3 · CHALLENGE-C

**Defect.** The generated palette — the workbench's entire output — has zero accessible representation, and none of the three verbs announces anything.

**Mechanism.** Every colour-bearing element is (correctly, individually) marked decorative, but no element ever supplies the text alternative at the level where the palette is the content — so a chain of locally-correct aria-hidden decisions sums to a workbench whose output does not exist for AT. Compounded by no aria-live for the async/replacing results.

**Evidence.**

```
PaletteColorStrip.vue:2-10 aria-hidden="true" role="presentation" · the swatches are producer-hardcoded aria-hidden with the :aria-label discarded (C-1) · PreviewStrip.vue:43 aria-hidden="true" · GenerateControls.vue:293-296 the count ramp is an unlabelled decorative div · LIVE probe of every live region on the page while operating the plate: [{"role":"alert","live":null,"text":"dev misconfigured — run `npm run dev`"}] — that is the dev banner; there is no status seat
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/generate with VoiceOver or any AT: tab through the plate — exactly 4 stops (Palette name, Regenerate, Save palette, Copy all colors), none naming a colour; press Regenerate — the entire palette silently replaces itself with no announcement, the only changed announceable text being the seed hex. WCAG 1.1.1 and 4.1.3.

**Proposed cure.** Keep the strip and dots decorative — they are ornament. Give the PALETTE a text alternative once, at region level: the <section>'s accessible name carries count + ordered colours, or a visually-hidden <ul> of the css strings that doubles as the keyboard seat for C-1's copy verbs (one structure, both cures). Add exactly one role="status" in the plate that regenerate, save and both copies write to — which also discharges C-6 and C-12.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-5 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The count slider's horizontal axis simultaneously encodes two incompatible quantities: palette index (0..n-1 mapped to 0..100% of the track) and cardinality (count 1..12 mapped to 0..100%). The resulting affordance is affirmatively misleading.

**Mechanism.** F3 — depiction inflation. The extract k-slider pattern was transplanted without its precondition: there, track position plausibly IS cluster index; here the axis is cardinality, so the two meanings cannot coexist on one rail.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:65-73 paints the track by index: `const pct = colors.length === 1 ? 50 : (i / (colors.length - 1)) * 100`. :297-307 drives the thumb by count, `:min="1" :max="12"`. Live measure at count=5: gradient stops at `0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%` while `thumbCentreFrac: 0.367` (aria-valuenow 5, min 1, max 12 → (5-1)/11 = 0.364). The five colours span the entire track while the thumb sits at 36.7% of it, so 63% of the track is painted with colours that already exist — reading as 'drag right to reach these'. The comment at :62-64 claims 'the instrument shows its own state'; it shows a second, contradictory state.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. Read `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('div[style*="linear-gradient"]')).backgroundImage` → stops at 0/25/50/75/100%. Read the slider: `document.querySelector('[role="slider"]').getAttribute('aria-valuenow')` → "5", min 1, max 12. Compute the thumb centre as a fraction of the gradient layer's rect → 0.367. The last colour sits at 100% of a track whose thumb is at 36.7%.

**Proposed cure.** Give the rail one unit. Either paint the track neutral and let it be a pure cardinality axis (the honest reading), or redefine the ramp's x-position to mean count. Then adopt the §5 domain-neutral axis composition whole — VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:104 names 'Generate count' explicitly and requires label, unit and reserved live value. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8.

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-10 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The route has no `InstrumentChassis`; it is housed in a default Card with a uniform flex stack, so the specimen, the model inspector and the commit action are given identical structural weight. The count instrument additionally has no visible label arm.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion. The uniform `gap-4` between the three regions is precisely why nothing on the plate reads as hierarchy: everything is one rhythm, so no region can be protagonist.

**Evidence.**

```
GeneratePane.vue:31 wraps the route body in `<Card tier="resting" class="pane-scroll-fade ...">`; :35 and GenerateControls.vue:119 are both `flex flex-col gap-4`. :219 is a `grid grid-cols-2` local grid. Canon: VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:46 binds Generate's outer housing to `InstrumentChassis`; OPTICAL-BENCH-COMPOSITIONS.md:41 requires 'P122 golden: WatercolorDot specimen 61.8033989%; model inspector 38.1966011%' and 'Landmark-neutral chassis; no nested flat Card/local grid'; VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:38 — 'Card ... is never the default page primitive.' Label defect measured live: `sliderRowText: "5"` (the count row's entire visible text is a bare numeral) versus `selectLabels: ["Preset","Harmony"]`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:104 names 'Generate count' in the one Slider composition and requires a label arm. Count is additionally triplicated: `countDepictions: ["DIV|badge-atom.inline-flex|5","LABEL|text-mono-small.font-bold|5"]` plus aria-valuenow.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. `document.querySelector('.pane-scroll-fade').className` shows the Card housing; there is no chassis element and no 61.8/38.2 split in the layout. For the label: `document.querySelector('[role="slider"]').closest('.flex.items-center.gap-2').innerText.trim()` → "5", while `[...document.querySelectorAll('.section-label')].map(e=>e.textContent.trim())` → ["Preset","Harmony"].

**Proposed cure.** Adopt `InstrumentChassis` `golden` with three named regions — specimen stage, model inspector, commit action — replacing the three equal siblings. Give the count rail the §5 axis composition whole (label, unit, reserved live value) as Preset and Harmony already have, and delete the badge duplicate from the specimen plate: it is a model INPUT value rendered as specimen metadata on a plate whose stated job is to own only its own chrome. BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (RC-4).

### `CHALLENGE-D — assume the design is flawed (visual truth, sta` · D-11 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Dark-mode chrome carries 44% more chroma than light-mode chrome, moving away from the constitutionally required 'restrained neutral pole' and biasing the perceived hue of the specimen sitting on it.

**Mechanism.** F4 — housing by accretion. The well token was not audited for neutrality in the dark scheme, so the dark arm inherited more warm chroma than the light arm it was derived from.

**Evidence.**

```
Measured plate `background-color`, converted to OKLCh — light: `oklab(0.913295 0.00550478 0.0130424)` → L 0.9133, C 0.0142, h 67.1 deg. Dark: `oklab(0.345295 0.0103877 0.0175526)` → L 0.3453, C 0.0204, h 59.4 deg. The dark well is the MORE tinted of the two. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:21 — 'Dark chrome uses the restrained neutral pole. Seed tint is forbidden outside the ambient field, active accent, WatercolorDot/specimen, and pastel Palettes lanes.' The plate is chrome — §2's tier-4 'specimen well: opaque/quiet neutral stage; the specimen supplies color'. Visible in safari-desktop-dark/generate.png and safari-mobile-dark/generate.png, where the plate reads warm brown/taupe rather than neutral.
```

**Reproduction.** Navigate to http://localhost:9000/#/generate. `const p=()=>getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('[data-generate-plate]')).backgroundColor; const r=document.documentElement; const light=p(); r.classList.add('dark'); const dark=p(); r.classList.remove('dark'); ({light,dark})` → light `oklab(0.913295 0.00550478 0.0130424)`, dark `oklab(0.345295 0.0103877 0.0175526)`; chroma sqrt(a^2+b^2) = 0.0142 vs 0.0204.

**Proposed cure.** Resolve the dark well to the restrained neutral pole so the specimen dots sit on an unbiased ground — this is the one surface in the product where colour fidelity IS the product. Fix at the token/root level, never as a per-instance override (owner edict 5). BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (RC-4).

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-5 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The editable plate title is inert — the typed name is discarded and every save is written as the literal 'Generated Palette', despite a source comment declaring the name to be load-bearing provenance FOR the save.

**Mechanism.** E — states specified in comments, never composed. TypeScript cannot catch it: a handler may declare fewer parameters than the emit supplies.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:44-50 comment ('the bench title is provenance FOR the save, never display-only chrome ... this emit is already truthful') and `defineEmits<{ save: [colors: string[], name: string] }>()`; :102-104 emits paletteName.value. Consumer GeneratePane.vue:14 `function onSave(colors: string[])` never declares `name`; :19 `pm.createPalette("Generated Palette", paletteColors)` hardcodes it. Runtime: set title to ZZ-PROBE-NAME, click [aria-label="Save palette"] -> `AFTER SAVE — names found in localStorage: [{"key":"color-palettes","names":["Generated Palette"]}]` and `visible palette names in Library: ["Generated Palette"]`.
```

**Reproduction.** node gc-probe3.mjs -> AFTER SAVE block; frame frames/save-name-drop-chromium.png.

**Proposed cure.** Rule the title once: either it is provenance and the pane wires the name through, or it is not a control and the plate identity is a static --type-subheading line. The half-state — a full editing affordance (hover-underline, focus ring, cursor-text, aria-label) for a value with no consumer — is the defect.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-6 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The plate chrome row wraps at the primary desktop viewport and does NOT wrap at half-width, inverting its authored responsive intent and manufacturing a ~340px void between the title and the count badge. This falsifies the prior pass's hypothesis that 200% zoom would compound the wrap.

**Mechanism.** D — flex-basis vs fluid type: the layout sits ~2px from its wrap threshold, so the font-size clamp decides the composition.

**Evidence.**

```
Measured regen.top − nameInput.top: 1440 WebKit +36.5px (wrapped, plate h=220); 1440 Chromium +37px (wrapped, h=220); 1440 RTL +37px; 720 −5px (ONE LINE, h=180); 320 +37px. Same 462px plate width in both the 1440 and 720 arms — the only variable is the fluid type scale (16.4px at 1440). Mechanism at :148 `flex-1 basis-[10rem]` = flex: 1 1 10rem; flex line-breaking uses the flex BASE size, so 160px title + badge + shrink-0 cluster exceeds the 438px content box; after the break flex-grow:1 inflates the title to 398px. Comment :139-142 claims 'at 390 the verbs settle onto their own right-aligned line'. Cost: 40px of plate height (220 vs 180) = 22% of the plate.
```

**Reproduction.** node gc-probe5.mjs; compare verbWrapDelta across the 1440 / 720 / 320 / RTL arms.

**Proposed cure.** Do not tune the flex basis. Remove the verb cluster from the plate (D-4) and the row dissolves; title, count and seed then collapse into the chassis identity line.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-8 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The count axis invents new slider mechanics — a gradient div stacked behind the Slider plus a per-instance override that neutralises the producer's track — which also kills the producer's own value-fill layer, so the value is carried by thumb position alone over a track that never changes.

Measured clipboard content after clicking [aria-label="Copy all colors"]: "oklch(71.504823561991% 0.150951768272 279.521794645116deg), oklch(69.202771743294% 0.167397465226 57.029558695154deg), o...". ### `CHALLENGE-D — design (assume the design is flawed)` · D-11 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Copy and Save have neither a success state nor a failure state. The producer returns a result and documents the destructuring idiom; both call sites discard it, so a denied clipboard and a successful write are indistinguishable.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:106-113 `await writeClipboard(...)` with the result discarded at both sites. Producer node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/composables/dom/useClipboard.d.ts:35-37: ' * boolean, for identical call ergonomics: `const { ok } = await writeClipboard(text)`.' / 'export declare function writeClipboard(text: string): Promise<CopyResult>;'. save() emits with no confirmation. Canon VISUAL-CONSTITUTION.md:101 §5 'Persistent operation state stays with the entity/workspace. A transient flourish may celebrate success but never carries the only truth'; PROPORTION-AUDIT.md PR-08 'Pending/failure/export/recovery truth only transient -> ADD-AFFORDANCE'. glass-ui 7 exports ./toast; unused.
```

**Reproduction.** Read GenerateControls.vue:106-113 against useClipboard.d.ts:35-37; the producer's documented `{ ok }` is never destructured.

**Proposed cure.** Adopt LabeledField/Toast: the operation result becomes durable state on the entity, per §5, in the same wave that rules the title (D-5).

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-4 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The remove chip has ZERO reserved geometry: it is absolutely positioned 28px outside the component's own 40px layout box, consumes the parent's entire gap-5, and paints across (and visibly cuts) the next section's rule. Selecting a stop therefore repaints another section's boundary.

Violates PROPORTION-AUDIT.md §5.3: 'Renderer, icon or touch footprints may reserve collision space only on the axis where collision exists.' ### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-5 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The stop swatch is a tautology and therefore carries no information: at a stop's own position the ramp already IS that colour, so a colour-filled dot painted on the ramp has zero figure-ground separation and reads as an empty ring. At working density the instrument is a row of holes. Selected state differs only by scale 1.25 and border-white vs border-white/80.

**Mechanism.** a specimen placed on a ground that is definitionally identical to it

**Evidence.**

```
Shipped audit shot docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/gradient.png (and the crop evidence/D05-rest-state-rings-desktop-light.png) — both terminal handles render as white rings with the rail showing through. evidence/D05-twelve-stops-empty-rings.png: twelve stops, twelve empty rings, no specimen anywhere; the selected 6th is findable only by measuring. Violates VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §4.1 'Selected, failed, pending, withdrawn and disabled states are never colour-only. Role, accessible name, state/value … are explicit.' Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:247 (background: linear-gradient(cssColor,cssColor) over the ramp), :236-240 (border-white vs border-white/80), :60-63 (scale ladder).
```

**Reproduction.** Open docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/visual/shots/safari-desktop-light/gradient.png; or node evidence/p3.mjs block T6 which produces evidence/D05-twelve-stops-empty-rings.png

**Proposed cure.** The constitutional geometry: the stop rank moves OFF the meniscus onto a quiet seat rail beneath it. Each seat is a geometric button on a neutral well carrying a WatercolorDot face (D-6), so the face reads as a specimen, the meniscus stays the unbroken 'continuous liquid-color rail' of §2, and selection is expressed by the seat rather than by 5px of scale.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-7 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The keyboard grammar implements a third of the specified one and the accessibility tree carries no state at all: Home, End, ArrowUp, ArrowDown, PageUp/Down and the Space-grab are no-ops; the handle has no role, no aria-valuenow/min/max, no aria-pressed, and its accessible name carries no ordinal. A screen-reader user can move a stop but can never learn which one is selected or how many exist.

**Mechanism.** a bespoke button impersonating a value control

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p2.mjs T4 (focus handle 0, press each key, read aria-labels): Home → no change; End → no change; ArrowUp → no change; ArrowDown → no change; PageUp → no change; Space → no change; ArrowRight → 'Gradient stop at 1%'; Shift+ArrowRight → 'Gradient stop at 11%'. evidence/p1.mjs attribute dump: `attrs: data-stop-id, type=button, aria-label="Gradient stop at 0%", class, style`, `role: null`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5.2 mandates 'Home=0%, End=100%; announce stop identity, percentage, ordinal' and Up/Down at the same signed step. Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:173-187 (only ArrowLeft/ArrowRight/Delete/Backspace/Escape), :234 (aria-label without ordinal).
```

**Reproduction.** node docs/tranches/V/megatranche/audit/components/wb-gradient-stopeditor/evidence/p2.mjs — block T4; and evidence/p1.mjs handles[].attrs

**Proposed cure.** Compose the producer axis rather than inventing mechanics — VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5: 'The domain-neutral axis composition sits over BI Slider … Gradient … adopt that one composition; feature waves own their domain arrangement, not new slider mechanics.' The seat then carries role=slider, aria-valuenow/min/max and an aria-valuetext of identity + percentage + ordinal, plus a real selected state.

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-8 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** The click-to-add affordance starves as the instrument fills: every handle's always-on invisible hit expander (24px fine / 44px coarse) masks the bar's add gesture and suppresses the hover ghost in that band, with no indication of why the affordance stopped working. At the rail ends it is absolute from the first frame.

**Mechanism.** one control's inflated operable target masking a second, different gesture on the same surface

**Evidence.**

```
evidence/p3.mjs T6, 12 stops on a 462px rail: `T6 addable rail {"totalPx":462,"addablePx":165,"pct":35.7}` — 64.3% of the rail can no longer mint a stop; `T6 neighbour gaps px [44.9,36.9,36.6,36.9,37,36.5,37,55,36.5,36.9,45.8]`. evidence/p2.mjs T1: a click at `bar.x + 3` leaves the stop count at 2 (`T1 after click at bar.x+3 {"n":2}`). Source: GradientStopEditor.vue:87 and :98 (target.closest("[data-stop-id]") guards), :374-391 (the expander this guard now covers).

### `CHALLENGE-D — design of demo/workbenches/gradient/GradientVi` · D-9 · CHALLENGE-D

**Defect.** Selection has no payload and the domain's central verb is missing: `selectedStopId` has no consumer outside the component that emits it, and a stop's COLOUR cannot be edited anywhere in the product except by hand-editing the CSS text area. The cssColor arm of updateStop is dead code. There is no numeric position entry either.

**Mechanism.** a selection mode invented for local styling before the thing it selects FOR existed

**Evidence.**

```
`grep -rn "selectedStopId" demo/` returns exactly three lines, all in GradientVisualizer.vue (51 defineModel, 140 v-model:selected-id, 144 @select assigning the same ref); GradientPane.vue never binds it. `grep -rn "updateStop" demo/` returns the definition (useGradientModel.ts:127, patch type Partial<Pick<GradientStop,"cssColor"|"position">>) and ONE call site, GradientVisualizer.vue:95 `updateStop(id, { position })`. VISUAL-CONSTITUTION §5 requires the global grammar 'select → tune → commit'; §7 Gradient requires 'explicit add/move/remove/numeric alternatives'. PROPORTION-AUDIT PR-07 is the open register row.
```

**Reproduction.** grep -rn "selectedStopId" demo/ ; grep -rn "updateStop" demo/

**Proposed cure.** Land the selected-stop inspector (colour + numeric position + remove) — which simultaneously cures D-3 and D-4 — or delete the selection state, the scale ladder and the chip and let the rail be a pure move surface. The present middle position pays the whole cost of selection (a mode, a destructive control, a z-index war) for none of its value.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** demo/ui/** is nineteen directories of pure re-export shims over @mkbabb/glass-ui — the forbidden legacy alias layer — and the subject file reaches one design system through three different specifier registers in twelve lines.

**Mechanism.** Migration stopped one step short of the root: the post-shadcn directory shells were kept alive as an alias surface instead of being deleted and consumers rewritten — exactly the back-compat shim the standing edict forbids by name. Consequence is a dual (triple) path to one package with no rule distinguishing them.

**Evidence.**

```
Classification loop over demo/ui/*/: 19/19 SHIM (alert avatar badge button card checkbox collapsible dialog dropdown-menu input label popover radio-group select separator skeleton slider switch tooltip). Whole file: `demo/ui/select/index.ts` = `export { Select, SelectTrigger, ... } from "@mkbabb/glass-ui";`. Subject: GenerateControls.vue:4-12 (shim ×4), :14 root barrel, :15 subpath. Demo-wide specifier census: 37 root-barrel vs 15 /dock, 11 /watercolor-dot, 9 /dark, 9 /aurora, 8 /dom.
```

**Reproduction.** `for d in demo/ui/*/; do cat $d/index.ts; done` — every file is a single re-export line from @mkbabb/glass-ui; zero real components remain.

```

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-5 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The demo's proof of the published subpath surface is not a proof: tsconfig.demo.json's hand-written `paths` mirror has drifted from package.json#exports and SHADOWS it, so five of the seven public specifiers never consult the exports map at all.

**Mechanism.** A hand-maintained copy of a generated truth. vite.config.ts derives its self-alias set FROM package.json#exports precisely so it "can never drift"; the typecheck half is hand-written and drifted. Because TS paths substitution pre-empts exports resolution, deleting "./color" from the exports map leaves the demo typecheck GREEN while every real consumer breaks — a false proof of the public API, plus 3 dangling targets.

**Evidence.**

```
exports (7): ./color ./value ./css ./easing ./math ./transform ./quantize. tsconfig.demo.json paths (8): . /color /parsing /math /easing /units /transform /quantize. `ls dist/index.d.ts dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts dist/subpaths/units.d.ts` → all three 'No such file or directory'. tsc --traceResolution, two imports in the SAME file demo/color-session/generate-color.ts: '/color' → "'paths' option is specified... Trying substitution './dist/subpaths/color.d.ts'... use it as a name resolution result" (no 'Entering conditional exports', no Package ID); '/css' → "Entering conditional exports. Matched 'exports' condition 'types'. Using 'exports' subpath './css'... with Package ID '@mkbabb/value.js/dist/subpaths/css.d.ts@4.0.0'". The tsconfig header asserts "the exports map is a CLOSED 8-key set" — it is a closed 7-key set with different names.
```

**Reproduction.** npx tsc -p tsconfig.demo.json --noEmit --traceResolution 2>&1 | grep -iE "value\.js/(css|color)'" -A 6  — shows the two divergent mechanisms; `ls dist/index.d.ts dist/subpaths/parsing.d.ts dist/subpaths/units.d.ts` exits 1 on all three.

**Proposed cure.** Delete the @mkbabb/value.js* paths block from tsconfig.demo.json outright — self-reference through package.json#exports already resolves (the /css trace proves it, with a real Package ID), so all seven subpaths would resolve through the genuine exports map and removing a subpath would turn the demo typecheck red, which is what a dogfood is for. If a paths block must survive, generate it from package.json#exports the way vite.config.ts does. CURABLE NOW.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The rail axis is mapped twice over two different boxes: the forward map (pointer -> position) is TypeScript over the BORDER box (getBoundingClientRect), while the inverse map (position -> pixels) is CSS calc where `100%` resolves against the PADDING box. They disagree by the 1px border at each end, so the two maps are not inverse.

**Mechanism.** one geometric law with two implementations in two languages over two coordinate origins; the CSS one silently changes meaning if a border or padding is ever added to .gradient-rail

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStopEditor.vue:75-81 (forward: origin rect.left=224, span rect.width-20=442) vs :55-57 (inverse: `calc(10px + (100% - 20px) * p/100)` where 100%=460, origin=225). LIVE measured: rail x=224.0 width=462.0 borderLeft=1px; handle "Gradient stop at 0%" style.left="calc(0% + 10px)" measured centre cx=235; handle "…100%" style.left="calc(100% - 10px)" cx=675. Forward map at those exact pixels: pos(235)=0.226, pos(675)=99.774. LIVE confirmation: clicking the bar at x=235 — the exact pixel where the 0% handle is painted, at y=railTop+4 so the bar owns the gesture — minted a stop at 0.2%, rendered `style.left="calc(0.2% + 9.96px)"`, cx=235.88.
```

**Reproduction.** http://localhost:9000/#/gradient -> in the console, click the rail at exactly `bar.getBoundingClientRect().x + 11` with clientY = rail top + 4 -> the new stop reads 0.2%, not 0%, and its handle lands ~0.9px right of the pointer. Error is antisymmetric: 0 at 50%, ±0.23% at the ends.

**Proposed cure.** A single useRailGeometry(el, halfPx) exposing toPosition(clientX) and toOffsetPx(position) over ONE box, built on clamp+scale from @mkbabb/value.js/math, with the handle positioned in px rather than calc(%) so the two directions are provably inverse. Ghost and handle then ride the same map by construction.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The self-declared "ONE sampling law" is implemented twice, and the model's interpolation space never reaches the emitted CSS. GradientVisualizer.colorAtPosition re-implements the interval walk / easing / mix that sampleCoalescedStops owns, then injects it into this component as an optional function prop — so the ghost dot and the rail it floats over are painted by two different implementations. Separately, rampGradient emits `linear-gradient(90deg, …)` with no `in <space>` clause, so the browser blends between sub-stops in sRGB while the model's law is oklch; COALESCE_RESOLUTION=32 exists to make that error small, i.e. the coalescing machinery is a polyfill for a CSS Color 4 feature the target browsers already ship.

**Mechanism.** the concept "the colour of this ramp at position p" has no exported home, so it leaks into a component prop; and the model's declared interpolation space is dropped at the serialization boundary, forcing a 32-sample approximation of an exact platform feature

**Evidence.**

```
useGradientCSS.ts:171-174 declares "The ONE sampling law"; useGradientCSS.ts:175-216 implements it; GradientVisualizer.vue:64-88 re-implements it and passes it as `:color-at` (GradientVisualizer.vue:139) into GradientStopEditor.vue:16. useGradientCSS.ts:223 `return \`linear-gradient(90deg, ${parts.join(", ")})\`` — no interpolation-space clause though model.interpolationSpace is in scope. Live computed --rail-ramp confirms: `linear-gradient(90deg, oklch(75% …) 80.00%, …)`, no `in oklch`.
```

**Reproduction.** Structural — two call sites, both quoted with file:line. The sRGB-vs-oklch deviation between adjacent sub-stops is bounded by the 32-sample density and was NOT separately measured: that half is labelled a HYPOTHESIS.

**Proposed cure.** model/sample.ts exports both sampleAt(model, position) -> AnyColor (the ghost AND the bar-click mint) and sampleRamp(model); model/serialize.ts emits `linear-gradient(<dir> in <space>, …)` and coalesces ONLY across intervals whose easing is non-linear. Since parseGradientCSS seeds every parsed interval to the `linear` preset (gradientParse.ts:295-298), the common case collapses to an exact two-stop `linear-gradient(in oklch, …)`. The colorAt prop is deleted.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-7 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The gradient domain TYPES live inside the state-factory module, so every module needing only a shape must import the factory — producing two type-graph cycles, and anchoring this component's prop contract on the cycle's apex.

**Mechanism.** types homed in a stateful module instead of a leaf; stated precisely — both cycles are TYPE-ONLY, so verbatimModuleSyntax erases them and the RUNTIME graph stays acyclic (model->css, model->parse->css). The declaration graph is cyclic, and it is why L-5's dead barrel cannot be deleted without first moving the types.

**Evidence.**

```
GradientStop/GradientType/GradientInterval/GradientModelState declared at useGradientModel.ts:33-62, in the same module that builds reactive state (73-193) and re-exports the serializers and parser (19-29). Edges: useGradientModel.ts:15 -> useGradientCSS (VALUE: useGradientCSS, linearInterval); useGradientCSS.ts:31-34 -> useGradientModel (TYPE) = cycle. useGradientModel.ts:16 -> gradientParse (VALUE: parseGradientCSS); gradientParse.ts:23-27 -> useGradientModel (TYPE) = cycle. GradientStopEditor.vue:4 imports type GradientStop from that apex.
```

**Reproduction.** Read the five import statements above; the cycle is visible in the declaration graph, not in the emitted JS.

**Proposed cure.** A leaf model/types.ts with zero intra-tree imports (only value.js and glass-ui TYPE imports) carrying GradientStop | GradientType | GradientInterval | GradientModelState | OrderedStops. Everything depends on the leaf; nothing depends on the factory except the Vue layer. GradientStopEditor.vue:4 becomes `import type { GradientStop } from "../model/types"` and stops transitively naming the parser, the serializers and vue's watch.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-6 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The shell→feature action seam is five hops of `any` and double optional-chain masking: the dock action bar reaches into this component's instance by name to invoke Regenerate/Save/Copy.

**Mechanism.** Ownership, not typing. useColorGeneration() is instantiated INSIDE GenerateControls (:35-42), so generation state is owned by a component instance and the shell has no way to reach it except by grabbing the instance. The `any` is the symptom; the state's home is the disease. The `?.` on the METHOD (as distinct from the instance) exists only to swallow a contract mismatch — a masking fallback in the sense edict 2 forbids: rename `regenerate` anywhere in the chain and the dock button silently no-ops with no type error, no runtime error, no console warning.

**Evidence.**

```
Chain: demo/color-picker/App.vue:317 `const generatePaneRef = ref<any>(null)` → demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:106-110 `interface PaneActionRefs { generate: Ref<any>; gradient: Ref<any>; mix: Ref<any> }` → usePaneRouter.ts:196-198 `handler: () => paneRefs.generate.value?.regenerate?.()` → GeneratePane.vue:23-25 `regenerate: () => controlsRef.value?.regenerate?.()` → GenerateControls.vue:115 `defineExpose({ regenerate, save, copyColors })`. Those three Ref<any> are the ONLY `any` in usePaneRouter.ts (`grep -n 'any' demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts` → lines 108, 109, 110 only).
```

**Reproduction.** Read the five sites above. The same three verbs are ALSO rendered declaratively on the plate (:157-184), so one concept has both a declarative and an imperative path and only the imperative one can break silently.

**Proposed cure.** Invert it. useColorGeneration becomes a pane-scoped session provided by GeneratePane under a typed InjectionKey — the pattern already used in this very tree (CSS_COLOR_KEY, LIBRARY_PORT_KEY, EDIT_TARGET_KEY). The pane declares its action descriptors as typed DATA ({key, icon, title, handler}[]) and hands them to the router, which injects rather than reaches. PaneActionRefs, both defineExpose blocks, controlsRef, App.vue's three ref<any>, and all six ?. masks delete. Pays three times: elegance, correctness (L-1 becomes unrepresentable — save reads the name from the same session the input writes), and performance (session computeds replace per-render template calls, L-9).

---

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L2-2 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The ramp-track slider is implemented twice, and the two copies diverged exactly at the WCAG 1.4.11 contrast cure: the extract twin carries an inset contrast ring and an O-18 census hook, the generate twin carries neither.

**Mechanism.** A design-system capability ('a Slider whose track paints a supplied ramp') implemented as a per-pane absolutely-positioned div sandwich plus `--slider-track-bg: transparent`. Copied code does not diverge at random — it diverges wherever only one copy received the fix. Upgrades pass-1 §L-10 from INFO to MAJOR.

**Evidence.**

```
Copy A: demo/workbenches/extract/composables/useExtractSession.ts:101-112 (kSliderGradient) + ExtractControls.vue:20-33 with `boxShadow: inset 0 0 0 1.5px ${trackInk}` (:22) and data-o18="extract-k-rail". Copy B: GenerateControls.vue:65-73 (countSliderGradient) + :292-307, no ring, no hook. Same algorithm line for line (`pct = n===1?50:(i/(n-1))*100`, `.toFixed(0)`, `"var(--muted)"` fallback); GenerateControls.vue:284-285 says so — 'the extract k-slider pattern verbatim'. MEASURED live rail: {"railBoxShadow":"none","railRect":{"w":434,"h":24},"railInlineBg":"background: linear-gradient(to right, oklch(0.63947 0.110159 190.777) 0%, …)"}. ORACLE ASYMMETRY: e2e/smoke/oracles/o18-contrast-census.spec.ts:1106,1126,1133 census extract-kc and extract-k-rail only; the generate rail appears in no census row. The cure's own provenance: ExtractControls.vue:59-64 ('the track re-inks with the CONTRACT … at the WCAG 1.4.11 graphics floor').
```

**Reproduction.** Playwright at /#/generate: read the rail div's computed boxShadow → "none"; compare ExtractControls.vue:22 which sets an inset 1.5px trackInk ring. `grep -rn 'data-o18' e2e/smoke/oracles/o18-contrast-census.spec.ts` → extract hooks only.

**Proposed cure.** Glass 8 gate G-2 (already filed) gains G-2′: the producer's ramp-track affordance MUST carry the WCAG 1.4.11 contrast ring, so a consumer cannot obtain the ramp without the ring. Both underlay divs, both `--slider-track-bg: transparent` overrides and both gradient builders then delete together. Independently of G-2′, add data-o18="generate-count-rail" plus its census row — that is the only mechanism that would have caught the divergence.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L2-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Two colour-harmony vocabularies live in one application: glass-ui 7 owns `ColorHarmony` (consumed by the Atmosphere pane) while the demo mints a rival `HARMONY_NAMES` with its own generator. The same concept ships under two spellings — 'Triadic' in Generate, 'triad' in Atmosphere.

**Mechanism.** Unique semantic ownership violated twice: the concept is absent from @mkbabb/value.js (the constellation's colour library), present in @mkbabb/glass-ui (a UI library — itself an inversion of dependency direction), and privately re-implemented a third time in demo/.

**Evidence.**

```
PRODUCER: node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/composables/color/index.d.ts:81 `export type ColorHarmony = "analogous"|"complementary"|"split-complementary"|"triad"|"tetradic"|"monochrome"`, :87 `deriveHue(anchorHue, harmony, hueSpread, t)`; documented at dist/components/aurora/composables/color.d.ts:29-34 as 'the PUBLIC alias of the shared ColorHarmony vocabulary … The blob and aurora derive from this ONE union.' DEMO RIVAL: demo/color-session/generate-color.ts:68-85 `HARMONY_NAMES = ["golden","analogous","complementary","triadic","split-complementary","random"]` with its own generateHues at :93-149. CONSUMERS: GenerateControls.vue:264-278 (+capitalize at :83-85) renders 'Triadic'; demo/scenes/atmosphere/AuroraPane.vue:121-127, typed AuroraHarmony=ColorHarmony via @mkbabb/glass-ui/aurora, renders the glass-ui union with 'triad'.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static, but exhaustively enumerated: the two unions, their two generators, and their two rendered menus are all cited at file:line above. The user-visible 'Triadic' vs 'triad' divergence is derivable from the two type declarations plus the two capitalisation paths.

**Proposed cure.** One vocabulary, one home, and the home is the colour library, not the component library. Fold ColorHarmony into pass-1's proposed `@mkbabb/value.js/palette` subpath and have glass-ui CONSUME it (colour vocabulary flowing library → design system, not the reverse); retire the demo's rival. RF-17 library-evolution wave — 4.0.0 is immutable, so this is a next-cut row, not a megatranche edit.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L2-4 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The design system's `Select` publishes no typed model-value, so four demo files reach past glass-ui into its own transitive dependency (reka-ui) and launder the enum boundary through nine unchecked `as` casts.

**Mechanism.** Wrong direction of dependency. The demo depends on the design system's PRIVATE choice of headless substrate; swap reka-ui in Glass 8 and four demo files break for a reason unrelated to their own concern. Pass 1 correctly noted reka-ui is a declared devDependency — declaredness is not the defect, direction is.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:33 `import type { AcceptableValue } from "reka-ui"`; :75-81 `preset.value = value as PresetName` / `harmony.value = value as HarmonyName`. `grep -c AcceptableValue node_modules/@mkbabb/glass-ui/dist/index.d.ts` → 0 (glass-ui does not re-export it). Census: GenerateControls.vue:33 (casts :76,:80); MixConfigBar.vue:15 (casts :99,:122,:146); GradientVisualizer.vue:28 (casts :164,:181,:198); AuroraPane.vue:25 (casts :79,:81,:85,:92) = 4 files / 9 casts.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static; the grep census above is exhaustive over demo/.

**Proposed cure.** Producer ask alongside G-1/G-2/G-3: `Select<T extends string>` with `@update:model-value: (v: T) => void` (or a re-export of the value type from the glass-ui root). Nine casts and four reka-ui imports die and the preset/harmony boundary becomes type-checked instead of asserted. Same lesson as G-3 from the other side: a design-system prop OR event whose type the consumer must guess is not a contract.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 (pass-1, independently censused by pass 2) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** Cross-feature edge: workbenches/generate imports PaletteColorStrip from the palettes feature's card cluster, dragging the palettes DOMAIN type into a feature that has no palette.

**Mechanism.** A presentational primitive filed inside a feature it does not belong to. 75% of its consumers are outside its home, so 3 features must cross a boundary and adapt to a domain type none of them own. Meanwhile the CORRECT shared home already exists and this same file already imports from it (demo/color-session/color-chips/index.ts:1-27 declares itself 'ONE focused common module for the multi-feature chip grammar, never a per-pane copy').

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:16 `import { PaletteColorStrip } from "../../palettes/browser/card"`. CENSUS — 4 consumers, 3 of 4 outside palettes/: MixSourceSelector.vue:8,203; GenerateControls.vue:16,135; ExtractWorkbench.vue:200; only PaletteCard.vue:33,175 is in-feature. LAW: docs/tranches/V/audit/REFORMATION-2026-07-16.md:73 (RF-15) enumerates the surviving true-shared buckets and names this species as work owed ('Cross-feature violation to fix: ConsoleRail → palette api barrel'). TAX: `grep -rn 'css, i) => ({' demo` → 4 synthesis sites (GenerateControls.vue:56, GeneratePane.vue:15, CurrentPaletteEditor.vue:244, useGradientModel.ts:144) fabricating `{css, position}` to satisfy props typed on PaletteColor (PaletteColorStrip.vue:29,35-45, reading c.weight; demo/palettes/types.ts:1-11 shows the faked name?/weight? fields).
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static; the consumer census above is exhaustive (`grep -rn 'PaletteColorStrip' demo`).

**Proposed cure.** Move the strip into the existing shared chip module as `demo/color-session/color-chips/ColorStrip.vue`, retyped domain-free: props { stops: readonly string[]; weights?: readonly number[]; orientation? }. PaletteCard maps PaletteColor[] → (stops, weights) at its own boundary, where the domain type belongs. Result: 3 cross-feature edges die, 2 synthesis adapters die, the domain type stops leaking, and all three strip forms sit under one declared grammar. No new directory minted (edict 3) — color-chips already exists and already claims this concept.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-2 (pass-1, independently measured by pass 2) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `demo/ui/` is a 19-directory alias-shim layer over the design system, and this single 312-line file consumes glass-ui through three different idioms at once.

**Mechanism.** Survival of the pre-glass-ui shadcn era: an alias layer preserving old import paths. Edict 2 (no aliases / migration shims / dual paths) and edict 3 (no wrapper indirection).

**Evidence.**

```
Measured: 19 demo/ui/*/index.ts barrels; 18 are one-line pure re-exports of @mkbabb/glass-ui (e.g. `demo/ui/button/index.ts` = `export { Button } from "@mkbabb/glass-ui";`), and the nineteenth (alert/) is also a pure re-export carrying only a comment. GenerateControls.vue:3-12 uses the shim (../../ui/select|slider|button|badge), :14 uses the root barrel (writeClipboard), :15 uses a subpath (@mkbabb/glass-ui/watercolor-dot). glass-ui 7.0.0 publishes ./badge ./button ./select ./slider among 74 exports and declares sideEffects: ["*.css"], so the root barrel tree-shakes as well as a subpath — the shim buys nothing, not even bundle size.
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static; `for d in demo/ui/*/; do …` shim census run in the report's command log.

**Proposed cure.** Delete demo/ui/ entirely; every call site imports the glass-ui subpath directly — the idiom this very file already uses for WatercolorDot. A clean measured down-metric for the RF-21 structure-settlement wave (19 directories, 19 files). Sequence after the Glass 8 unblock, since it touches the pinned file's import block.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-4/L2-6 (library ownership asymmetry) · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The generation core is stranded in the demo while its sibling workbench's core is a published library subpath — the same tree, the same shape of pure colour math, opposite sides of the src/↔demo/ line.

**Mechanism.** Arbitrary placement of a library-shaped capability. `generatePalette(count, preset, harmony, seed) → string[]` is pure, deterministic, gamut-mapped and DOM-free — exactly the shape of a public subpath export. An external consumer of @mkbabb/value.js gets quantizePixels but must re-implement harmony generation from scratch; the demo cannot dogfood the public surface for its own headline feature.

**Evidence.**

```
EXTRACT'S MATH IS THE LIBRARY: src/subpaths/quantize.ts exports dominantColor/quantizePixels, consumed at demo/workbenches/extract/quantize-worker.ts:6-7, useExtractSession.ts:14, useImageQuantize.ts:9 via `@mkbabb/value.js/quantize`. GENERATE'S IS NOT: demo/color-session/generate-color.ts (243 lines, pure, seeded, zero Vue) + demo/color-session/prng.ts (mulberry32), touching the library only for primitives at :33 (oklch, mapColorToGamut from /color) and :34 (serializeCssColor from /css).
```

**Reproduction.** NONE — static; the subpath census (`grep -rhno '@mkbabb/value\.js[a-z/.]*' demo` → 25 /color, 10 /css, 6 /math, 5 /easing, 4 /quantize, 0 bare) and the two file trees are cited above.

**Proposed cure.** Relocate generatePalette/generateSingleColor/GENERATION_PRESETS/the harmony union into a new `@mkbabb/value.js/palette` subpath (with mulberry32 to src/math/prng.ts), and have glass-ui's ColorHarmony re-point at it (see L2-3). RF-17 library-evolution wave — 4.0.0 is immutable.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-3 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The public surface parses CSS timing functions but cannot serialize one, so the demo hand-mints the literal and must stay byte-identical with a private glass-ui implementation — held in agreement by a comment.

**Mechanism.** asymmetric public surface (parse without serialize) forces a duplicate implementation of one CSS serialization law into a consumer, coupled to the original only by prose.

**Evidence.**

```
`grep -rn "export function serialize|export const serialize" src/css/ src/easing.ts` → serializeCssColor, serializeKeyframeSelector, serializeCssValue, serializeTimelineOptions — no timing-function serializer, while `/css` exports `parseTimingFunction` (src/subpaths/css.ts). Consequence at easingCatalogue.ts:38-56: comment says "glass-ui `useEasingPicker.readout` mints … MUST be byte-identical", then `bezierLiteral` re-implements `+n.toFixed(3)` joined by `", "` and `stepsLiteral` re-implements `steps(${n}, ${term})`.
```

**Reproduction.** Deterministic from source: change glass-ui's `readout` join from `", "` to `","`. `bezierLiteral` still emits `", "`. The identity match at easingCatalogue.ts:220 (`SPECIMEN_TILES.find(t => t.css === interval.css)`) is a string compare, so it returns undefined, `tileIdFor` returns null, `specimenNameFor` (line 229) returns "custom" — all 30 bezier tiles render unpressed and every closed specimen row's head reads `custom` for a named preset, with no error raised anywhere.

**Proposed cure.** Add `serializeTimingFunction(ast: CssTimingFunction): string` to `src/css/`, exported from `/css` as the exact inverse of `parseTimingFunction`, round-trip tested in the existing test/bbnf-equivalence idiom. `bezierLiteral`/`stepsLiteral` delete; glass-ui's private minter delegates. One law, one home, provable by round-trip instead of by comment.
