### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-10 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** "Palette stops → CSS rail" is hand-rolled in four places with no owner, the markup sandwich is duplicated verbatim across two workbenches, and the mechanism violates the shared chip module's own SAMPLING LAW — visibly, in the same card.

**Mechanism.** The concept "a slider whose track IS a color ramp" has no home, so it lives four times in demo features and zero times in the design system that owns Slider. The component then runs TWO contradictory mechanisms for "show a color sequence" — the law-abiding library-sampled PreviewStrip in its dropdowns (:245, :274) and the forbidden raw CSS gradient in its slider track (:72), eleven lines above the import of the module that forbids it.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:65-73 and demo/workbenches/extract/composables/useExtractSession.ts:101-112 are near-byte twins: same `length===0 → "var(--muted)"` guard, same `length===1?50:(i/(len-1))*100`, same `.toFixed(0)`, same `linear-gradient(to right, ${stops.join(", ")})`. Two more copies at demo/workbenches/mix/MixResultDisplay.vue:112 and demo/picker/controls/ComponentSliders/ComponentSliders.vue:195. The MARKUP is duplicated too: GenerateControls.vue:292-307 vs ExtractControls.vue:18-33 are character-identical on the structural lines (`relative flex-1 h-6 flex items-center` wrapper, `absolute inset-0 rounded-full overflow-hidden h-6` rail div, `Slider variant="spectrum" class="relative w-full"`, `:style="{ '--slider-track-bg': 'transparent' }"`). That last line is a per-instance override of a design-system token, which edict 5 forbids by name; `grep -rn -- '--slider-track-bg' demo/` finds 4 override sites. demo/color-session/color-chips/sample.ts:5-12 legislates the opposite: "ramps are k-sample discrete stops built from THE LIBRARY's mixColors interpolation — NEVER CSS `in <space>` gradient interpolation, because the preview must show what THE APP computes, not what the browser's engine would … One mechanism for all rows, no engine divergence."
```

**Reproduction.** Visible in shots/safari-desktop-light/generate.png: the plate strip shows five clean hard segments (green·lavender·gold·cyan·pink) while the count slider directly below shows the browser's sRGB interpolation between them — the muddy grey-green between green and lavender is a color the app never computed. The instrument and its readout disagree in the same card. Confirm the duplication with `grep -rn 'linear-gradient(to right' demo/`.

**Proposed cure.** BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (G-2): the rail belongs in glass-ui as a first-class affordance on the existing Slider — a `trackStops: readonly string[]` prop or a `track` slot — with sampling routed through color-chips/sample.ts so the slider obeys the same SAMPLING LAW as the chips and the visible engine divergence disappears. Both workbenches then delete their rail div, their --slider-track-bg override, and their gradient computed; the whole idiom collapses to `<Slider :track-stops="stops" />`.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-11 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** `.generate-swatch` is a per-component class hook whose only rules are hand-added to two global selector lists that already carry the general seam `[data-color-surface]`.

**Mechanism.** Cross-cutting media rules do belong in a global sheet (edict 6 is not violated — nothing was deleted); the defect is shape. Because the component's swatches do not carry the existing general attribute, two more class names had to be appended to two separate hand-maintained lists, and the next color-painting component will require a third append.

**Evidence.**

```
GenerateControls.vue:205 sets class="generate-swatch …"; the file has no <style> block (`grep -c '<style' GenerateControls.vue` → 0). Its only rules live in demo/styles/foundation.css:689 and :834, inside @media (forced-colors: active) and @media print, enumerated alongside `.swatch-row > *`, `.shadow-swatch`, `.watercolor-swatch` and — crucially — `[data-color-surface]`, the attribute hook that already expresses the concept.
```

**Reproduction.** `grep -rn 'generate-swatch' demo/` → 1 consumer (GenerateControls.vue:205), 2 global rule lists (foundation.css:689, :834); read both blocks and observe [data-color-surface] already present in each.

**Proposed cure.** BLOCKED-ON-GLASS-V8 (G-4): have WatercolorDot carry data-color-surface — a producer one-liner, since the dot is a glass-ui primitive. Both global lists then shrink to the attribute selector alone, and .generate-swatch deletes along with .shadow-swatch and .watercolor-swatch.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-12 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The composable's docstring cites `@composables/color/generate-color`, a path alias W43 (RF-15) deleted.

**Mechanism.** Comment decay. Trivial in isolation, but it is the third stale prose assertion in this component's closure — with L-5's "CLOSED 8-key set" comment and L-8's "at 390" wrap comment. The component is heavily commented and those comments are load-bearing for the next author, so their decay rate is itself a structural risk here.

**Evidence.**

```
demo/workbenches/generate/composables/useColorGeneration.ts:8 cites `@composables/color/generate-color`. tsconfig.demo.json:32-34 records the kill: "the demo `@…` path aliases were killed … No @styles/@components/@utils/@lib/@composables/@assets project alias survives." The real import two lines below is `../../../color-session/generate-color`.
```

**Reproduction.** Read useColorGeneration.ts:8 against its own import at :17, and against tsconfig.demo.json:32-34.

**Proposed cure.** CURABLE NOW (outside the pin — the file is composables/useColorGeneration.ts, not GenerateControls.vue): cite the real relative path. More durably, prefer structural expression over prose: the layering claims these comments make should be enforced by an import-boundary lint rule, not asserted in text that no tool checks.

### `CHALLENGE-L — library structure (module boundaries, ownershi` · L-12 · CHALLENGE-L

**Defect.** The specimen catalogue is built as a module-scope side effect, so 1,617 curve evaluations and 22.6 KB of retained SVG path strings are paid on gradient-pane open whether or not any easing row is ever expanded.

**Mechanism.** eager module-scope evaluation of derived data in a leaf module — the catalogue is a pure function of the frozen library constant bezierPresets but is neither lazy nor build-time generated.

**Evidence.**

```
easingCatalogue.ts:198 `export const SPECIMEN_FAMILIES: SpecimenFamily[] = buildFamilies();` — top level. Static import chain: easingCatalogue → GradientEasingEditor.vue:35 → GradientVisualizer.vue:16 → async GradientPane (demo/shell/usePaneRouter.ts:74). Measured against this repo's dist/subpaths/easing.js: tiles 33, build 3.02 ms cold / 1.77 ms avg warm, 22,651 bytes of path strings retained. glyphPath samples 49 points per curve (samples=48, i<=samples) with two toFixed(3) formats each → 1,617 evaluations, ~3,234 string formats.
```

**Reproduction.** `node scratchpad/cat-bench.mjs` (the exact catalogue arithmetic against dist/subpaths/easing.js) → `tiles: 33 / build ms (cold): 3.02 / path-string bytes retained: 22651 / avg ms over 10 warm builds: 1.77`.

**Proposed cure.** Make SPECIMEN_FAMILIES a lazily-built module singleton (getter memoized on first strip mount), or generate it at build time as static data — it is a pure function of a frozen library constant, so both are free.
